\geq 0$ we get contradiction to clause $(\gamma)$ above. If $\beta=0$ 
we use clause (i) to copy the situation to $M$ and get a contradiction. 

\noindent
2) Similar proof.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- notation-1: body_md -->
\label{2:5}
Let $\bar\lambda$ denote a finite (or countable) sequence of pairs of infinite 
cardinals $\langle (\lambda_\zeta;\kappa_\zeta):
\zeta < \zeta(*)\rangle$ such that $\kappa_\zeta$ increases with 
$\zeta$, so e.g. $\bar\lambda^{\oplus}= \langle(\lambda^\oplus_\zeta,
\kappa^\oplus_\zeta): \zeta <\zeta^\oplus(*)\rangle$. 
We shall identify a strictly increasing $\bar\kappa = \langle 
\kappa_\zeta:\zeta \le \zeta(*)\rangle$ with $\langle(\kappa_{\zeta+1};
\kappa_\zeta):\zeta< \zeta(*)\rangle$. 

Let $R,R_0,Q_0,\dots, R_{\zeta(*)-1}, Q_{\zeta(*)-1}$ be fixed unary 
predicates and $\bar R=\langle(R_\zeta, Q_\zeta):
\zeta<\zeta(*)\rangle$.

A $\bar\lambda$-model $M$ is a model $M$ such that: $R$,
$R_\zeta, Q_\zeta \in \tau(M)$ are all unary predicates,
$|R^M_\zeta|=\lambda_\zeta$, $|Q^M_\zeta|=\kappa_\zeta$ for
$\zeta<\zeta(*)$, $Q^M_\zeta \subseteq R^M_\zeta$ and $\langle
R^M_\zeta:\zeta< \zeta (*) \rangle$ are pairwise disjoint, and
$R^M = \bigcup\limits_{\zeta<\zeta(*)}R^M_\zeta$. 

For $a \in R^M$ let $\zeta(a)$ be the $\zeta$ such that 
$a \in R^M_\zeta$ (e.g. $R^M_\zeta = \lambda_\zeta 
\backslash \bigcup\limits_{\xi<\zeta} \lambda_\xi$,
$\kappa_{\zeta+1} = \lambda_\zeta$). For a $\bar\lambda$-model $M$ 
we say that $a \in c \ell_\kappa(A,M)$ if $A \cup \{a\} \subseteq M$ 
and for some $n < \omega$, and quantifier free formula 
$\varphi(x_1,y_1,\dots,y_n)$ and $b_1,\dots, b_n \in A$ we have   

\[
M \models \varphi(a,b_1,\dots,b_n) \text{ and } (\exists^{\le\kappa} x) 
\varphi(x,b_1,\dots,b_n).
\]
<!-- end notation-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\label{2.6}
1)  For $\ell<6$, $\bar\lambda$ as in Notation \ref{2:5}, and an ordinal
$\alpha$ let $\Pr^\ell_\alpha(\bar\lambda;\theta)$ mean that for 
every $\bar\lambda$-model $M$, with $|\tau(M)|\le \theta$ we have 
$\mathrm{rk}^\ell(M,\bar\lambda) \ge \alpha$ (and 
$\mathrm{NPr}^l_\alpha(\bar\lambda,\theta)$ is the negation, if 
$\theta$ is omitted it means $\kappa_0$, remember $\bar\lambda=
\langle (\lambda_\zeta,\kappa_\zeta): \zeta<\zeta(*)\rangle$)
where  the rank is defined in part (2) below.

\noindent
2) For a $\bar \lambda$-model $M$, $\mathrm{rk}^\ell (M,\bar\lambda)=
\sup\{\mathrm{rk}^\ell(w,M,\bar\lambda)+1:\;w \in [\bar R^M]^*\}$ where the rank is
defined in part (3) below and:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(\alpha)$]   if $\zeta(*)$ is finite,
$[\bar R^M]^* = \{w:w$ a finite subset of $R^M$ not disjoint to any 
$R^M_\zeta\}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\beta)$]  if $\zeta(*)$ is infinite,
$[\bar R^M]^* = \{w: w$ a finite non-empty subset of $R^M\}$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
3)  For a $\bar\lambda$-model $M$, and $w \in [R^M]^*$ we define 
the truth value of ``$\mathrm{rk}^\ell(w,M;\bar\lambda) \ge \alpha"$ 
by induction on $\alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case A}:   $\alpha=0$

$\mathrm{rk}^\ell(w,M;\bar\lambda) \ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} no 
$a \in w \cap R^M$ belongs to 
$c \ell_{\kappa_{\zeta(a)}} (w\backslash\{a\},M)$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case B}:  $\alpha$ is a limit ordinal

$\mathrm{rk}^\ell(w,M;\bar\lambda) \ge \alpha $\underline{iff}
$\mathrm{rk}^\ell(w,,M;\bar\lambda) \ge \beta$ for every ordinal $\beta < \alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case C}:   $\alpha=\beta +1$

We demand two conditions:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(\alpha)$] exactly as in Definition \ref{1.1}(3)$(*)_3$ except
that when $\ell=2,3,4,5$ we use $\kappa =\kappa^+_{\zeta(a_k)}$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\beta)$]  if $\zeta< \zeta(*)$ and $w\cap R^M_\zeta=\varnothing$
then for some $a\in  R^M_\zeta,\mathrm{rk}^\ell (w\cup \{a\},M;\theta) \ge \beta$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- claim-8: body_md -->
\label{2.7}
The parallel of the following holds: \ref{1.2} (+ statements in
\ref{1.1}) also \ref{1.3} (use $\alpha<\kappa^+_0$), \ref{1.4}(2) 
(for $\alpha \le \kappa^+_0$), \ref{1.8}
(satisfying $\kappa^+_0$-c.c.) and we adapt \ref{1.5}.
<!-- end claim-8 -->
<!-- claim-9: body_md -->
\label{2.8}
If $\alpha$ is a limit ordinal and $\lambda_\xi \ge \beth_\alpha (\kappa_\xi)$
for every $\xi <\xi(*)$, \underline{then} $\Pr_\alpha (\bar\lambda)$.
<!-- end claim-9 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
se indiscernibility and Erd\H{o}s-Rado as in the proof of \ref{1.7}(1).

In more details.
The induction hypothesis on $\alpha$ is, assuming $\zeta(*) < \omega$:
if $A\subseteq R^M,\bigwedge\limits_{\zeta< \zeta(*)} |A \cap
R^M_\zeta| \ge \beth_{\omega\times \alpha}$ then for every 
$\beta<\alpha$, $k<\omega$
and every $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mum\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\langle m_\zeta: \zeta<\zeta(*)\rangle$, $m_\zeta\in
(0,\omega)$ for some $w \subseteq A$ we have
$\bigwedge\limits_{\zeta}|w \cap R^M_\zeta|= m_\zeta$ and 
$\mathrm{rk}(w;M,\bar\lambda) \ge \omega \times \beta+k$. Then for 
$\alpha=\gamma+1$, choose distinct $a^\zeta_i\in A\cap R^M_\zeta$ 
($i<\beth_{\omega\times\gamma+m+ m_\zeta}$) and use polarized partition
(see Erd\H{o}s, Hajnal, Mate, Rado \cite{EHMR}) on
$\langle\langle a^\zeta_i: i< \beth_{\omega \times \alpha}\rangle:
\zeta<\zeta(*)\rangle$. For $\zeta(*)$ infinite use $A\subseteq R^M$
such that $w_A=\{\zeta: A\cap R^M_\zeta \ne \varnothing\}$ is finite
non-empty, $\zeta\in w_A \Rightarrow |A\cap R^M_\zeta| \ge
\beth_{\omega \times \alpha}$ and proceed as above.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- claim-10: body_md -->
\label{2.9}
Let $\zeta(*) < \omega$, $\kappa^\varepsilon_0< \dots < \kappa^\varepsilon_{\zeta(*)}$,
$\bar\lambda^\varepsilon =\langle (\kappa^\varepsilon_{\xi+1},\kappa^\varepsilon_\xi):
\xi < \zeta(*)\rangle$ (for $\varepsilon \le \omega$).

\noindent
1)  If $\Pr_n(\bar\lambda^n)$ for $n < \omega$, and for some 
$\theta \le \kappa^\omega_0$ there is a tree $\mathscr{T} \in {}^{\theta >}
(\kappa^\omega_0)$ of  cardinality $\le \kappa^\omega_0$ with 
$\ge \kappa^\omega_{\zeta (*)}$ $\theta$-branches then: 
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes$]   every first order sentence which has a 
$\bar\lambda^n$-model for each $n$, also has a
$\bar\lambda^\omega$-model
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$\otimes'$]   moreover, if $\mathbf{T}$ is a first order 
theory of cardinality $\le \kappa^\omega_{0}$ and every finite 
$\mathbf{T}' \subseteq \mathbf{T}$ has a $\bar\lambda^n$-model for each 
$n$ \underline{then} $\mathbf{T}$ has a $\bar\lambda^\omega$-model.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
2)  So if $\bar\lambda^\varepsilon =\bar\lambda$ for $\varepsilon \le \omega$ 
are as \underline{above} then we have $\kappa^\omega_0$-compactness
for the class of $\bar\lambda^\omega$-models.
Where
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\oplus$]   a class $\mathfrak{K}$ of models is $\kappa$-compact when for
every set $\mathbf{T}$ of $\le \kappa$ first order sentences, if every finite
subset of $\mathbf{T}$ has a model in $\mathfrak{K}$ then $\mathbf{T}$ has a model in $\mathfrak{K}$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
3)  In part (1) we can use $\bar\lambda^n$ with domain 
$\omega_n$, if $\omega_n \subseteq \omega_{n+1}$ 
and $\zeta (*)=\bigcup \{w_n : n < \omega\}$.
<!-- end claim-10 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
traight if you have read \cite{Sh:8}, \cite{Sh:18}, \cite{Sh:37}
or read the proof of \ref{2.10} below (only that now the theory is 
not necessary countable, no types omitted, and by compactness it is 
enough to deal with the case $\zeta(*)$ is finite).
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- claim-11: body_md -->
\label{2.10}
Let $\zeta(*)< \omega_1$, $\bar\lambda^\varepsilon = \langle
(\kappa^\varepsilon_{\xi+1}, \kappa^\varepsilon_\xi): \xi<
\zeta(*)\rangle$ for each $\varepsilon \le \omega_1$ (and
$\kappa^\varepsilon_\xi$ strictly increasing with $\xi$).
 If $\Pr_\varepsilon (\bar\lambda^\varepsilon)$ for every
$\varepsilon< \omega_1$ and $\kappa^{\omega_1}_\xi \le 2^{\aleph_0}$ and
$\psi\in L_{\omega_1, \omega}$ and for each $\varepsilon< \omega_1$
there is a $\bar \lambda^\varepsilon$-model satisfying $\psi$ \underline{then}
there is a $\bar\lambda^{\omega_1}$-model satisfying $\psi$.
<!-- end claim-11 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
or simplicity, again like \cite{Sh:8}, \cite{Sh:18}, \cite{Sh:37}. Let
$M_\varepsilon$ be a $\bar \lambda^\varepsilon$ model of $\psi$ for
$\varepsilon< \omega_1$. By expanding the $M_\varepsilon$'s, by a
pairing function and giving names of subformulas of $\psi$ we have a
countable first order theory $\mathbf{T}$ with Skolem functions, a countable
set $\Gamma$ of $1$-types and $M^+_\varepsilon$ such that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]   $M^+_\varepsilon$ is a  $\bar \lambda^\varepsilon$-model of
$\mathbf{T}$ omitting each $p\in \Gamma$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(b)]   if $M$ is a model of $\mathbf{T}$ omitting every 
$p \in \Gamma$ \underline{then} $M$ is a model of $\psi$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Now as in the proof of \ref{2.1} we can find a model $M^+$ and
$a^\zeta_\eta$ for $\zeta< \zeta(*)$, $\eta\in {}^\omega 2$ such that
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(\alpha)$]   $M^+$ a model of $\mathbf{T}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\beta)$]   $a^\zeta_\eta\in R^M_\zeta$ and $\eta \ne \nu
\Rightarrow a^\zeta_\eta \ne a^\zeta_\nu$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\gamma)$] for every first order formula $\varphi(x_0,\ldots,
x_{n-1})\in L_{\omega, \omega}(\tau(\mathbf{T}))$
and $\zeta(0), \ldots$, $\zeta(n-1)< \zeta(*)$ ordinals, there is
$k< \omega$ such that: if $\eta_0, \ldots, \eta_{n-1}\in {}^\omega 2$,
$\nu_0, \ldots, \nu_{n-1}\in {}^\omega 2$ and $\langle \eta_\ell
\restriction k: \ell< n\rangle$ is with no repetitions and
$\eta_\ell\restriction k= \nu_\ell \restriction k$ {\em then} $M^+
\models \varphi (a^{\zeta(0)}_{\eta_0}, \ldots,
a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\eta_{n-1}}) \equiv \varphi (a^{\zeta(0)}_{\nu_0},
\ldots, a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\eta_{n-1}})$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\delta)$]   if $\sigma (x_0,\ldots,x_{n-1})$ is a term of $\tau
(\mathbf{T})$ and $\zeta(0), \ldots, \zeta(n-1) < \zeta(*)$, and $p\in \Gamma$
\underline{then} for some $k< \omega$ for any $\rho_0,\ldots,\rho(n-1)\in {}^k
2$ pairwise distinct there is $\varphi(x)\in p(x)$ such that:
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)$]  $\rho_\ell \triangleleft \eta_\ell \in {}^\omega 2
\Rightarrow M^+ \models \neg \varphi (a^{\zeta(0)}_{\eta_0}, \ldots,
a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\eta_{n-1}})$
\newline
(i.e. this is our way to omit the types in $\Gamma$)
\end{enumerate}
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\varepsilon)$]  if $\zeta(0), \ldots, \zeta(n-1) < \zeta(*)$,
$\sigma( x_0, \ldots, x_{n-1})$ is a term of $\tau(\mathbf{T})$, and $m<n$,
\underline{then}  for some $k< \omega$, we have
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)$]  if $\eta_0, \ldots, \eta_{n-1}\in {}^\omega 2$, and  $\nu_0,
\ldots, \nu_{n-1}\in {}^\omega 2$ and $\eta_\ell \restriction k =
\nu_\ell \restriction k$ and $\langle \eta_\ell \restriction k: \ell<
\omega \rangle$ is without repetitions and $\zeta(\ell)  < \zeta(m)
\Rightarrow \eta_\ell = \nu_\ell$
then $M^+ \models Q_{\zeta(m)} (\sigma(a^{\zeta(0)}_{\eta_0},
\ldots, a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\eta_{n-1}}))$ and 
$Q_{\zeta(m)}(\sigma(a^{\zeta(0)}_{\nu_0},\ldots,
a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\nu_{n-1}}) \Rightarrow 
\sigma(a^{\zeta(0)}_{\eta_0},\ldots,a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\eta_{n-1}}) =
\sigma (a^{\zeta(0)}_{\nu_0},\ldots,a^{\zeta(n-1)}_{\nu_{n-1}})$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Now choose $Y_\zeta \subseteq {}^\omega 2$ of cardinality
$\lambda^{\omega_1}_\zeta$ and let $M^*$ be the $\tau(M)$-reduct of
the Skolem hull in $M^+$ of $\{a^\zeta_\eta:\zeta< \zeta(*)$ and $\eta\in
Y_\zeta\}$. This is a model as required.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- conclusion-5: body_md -->
\label{2.11}
If $\mathbf{V}_0 \models \mathrm{GCH},\mathbf{V} = \mathbf{V}^{\mathbf{P}}_0$ for 
some c.c.c. forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$ then e.g.
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)$]   if $\aleph_{\omega \times 3} < 2^{\aleph_0},
\langle \aleph_0,\aleph_\omega,\aleph_{\omega + \omega}\rangle
\Rightarrow \langle \aleph_\omega,\aleph_{\omega +\omega},
\aleph_{\omega + \omega +\omega}\rangle $ (see \cite{Sh:8}) i.e., letting 
$\bar \lambda^0=\langle (\aleph_0,\aleph_\omega),(\aleph_\omega,
\aleph_{\omega+\omega})\rangle$ and $\bar\lambda^1 = 
\langle(\aleph_\omega,\aleph_{\omega+\omega}),(\aleph_{\omega+\omega},
\aleph_{\omega+\omega+\omega})\rangle$, for any
countable first order $\mathbf{T}$, if every finite $\mathbf{T}' 
\subseteq \mathbf{T}$ has a $\bar\lambda^0$-model then 
$\mathbf{T}$ has a $\bar\lambda^1$-model
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(**)$]   $\langle \kappa_\xi:\xi \le \xi(*) \rangle \rightarrow \langle
\kappa'_\xi:\xi \le \xi(*) \rangle$ if $\bigwedge\limits_\xi 
\kappa^{+\omega}_\xi \le \kappa_{\xi+1}$ and $\kappa'_0 \le 
\kappa'_1 \le \dots \le \kappa'{\xi(*)} \le 2^{\aleph_0}$ 
(and versions like \ref{2.9}(1)).
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end conclusion-5 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
hy?  By \ref{2.8} if $\bar \lambda=\langle (\lambda_\xi, \kappa_\xi):
\xi< \zeta(*)\rangle$, $\lambda_\xi \ge \beth_\omega(\kappa_\xi)$ 
then $\Pr_n(\bar\lambda)$ (really $\lambda_\xi \ge
\beth_k(\kappa_\xi)$ for $k$ depending on $n$ only suffices, see \cite{EHMR}).
Now ccc forcing preserves this and now apply \ref{2.9}.
Similarly we can use $\theta^+$-cc forcing $\mathbf{P}$ and deal with cardinals
in the interval $(\theta, 2^\theta)$ in $\mathbf{V}^\mathbf{P}$.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{2.12}
We can say parallel things for the compactness of $(\exists^{\ge
\lambda})$, for $\lambda$ singular $\le 2^{\lambda_0}$ (or $\theta +|\mathscr{T}| 
<\lambda \le$ number of $\theta$-branches of $\mathscr{T}$), e.g. we get the parallel 
of \ref{2.11}.

In more details, if $\mathbf{V}_0 = \mathbf{V}^\mathbf{P},\mathbf{P}$ satisfies the
$\theta^+$-c.c. then
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)$]   in $\mathbf{V}_0^\mathbf{P}$, for any singular $\lambda\in (\theta,
2^\theta)$ such that $\mathbf{V}_0 \models ``\lambda$ is strong limit" we have
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\circledast$]  the class $\{(\lambda,<,R_\zeta \ldots)_{\zeta<
    \theta}: R_\zeta$ an $n_\zeta$-relation, $(\lambda,<)$ is 
$\lambda$-like$\}$ of models is $\theta$-compact, and we can axiomatize it.
\end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
There are also consistent counterexamples, see \cite{Sh:532}.

The point of proving $(*)$ is:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes$]  for a vocabulary $\tau$ of cardinality $\le \theta$,
letting $\mathbf{T}^{\mathrm{sk}}_\tau$ be a first order theory with Skolem functions
$\tau(T^{\mathrm{sk}}_\tau)$ (but the Skolem functions are new), then TFAE for a
first order $\mathbf{T} \subseteq L_{\omega,\omega}(\tau)$
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]   $\mathbf{T}$ has a $\lambda$-like model
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(b)]   the following is consistent:
$\mathbf{T} \cup \mathbf{T}^{\mathrm{sk}}_\tau \cup \{\sigma(\ldots,x^{n(\ell)}_{\eta_\ell},
y_{n(\ell)},\ldots)_{\ell< k} = \sigma(\ldots,x^{n(\ell)}_{\nu_\ell},
y_{n(\ell)},\ldots)_{\ell < k} \vee
\sigma(\ldots,x^{n(\ell)}_{\eta_\ell},y_{n(\ell)} \ldots) > y_n:
n(\ell)< \omega$, $\eta_\ell \in {}^\omega 2$, and for some $j<\omega$,
$\langle \eta_\ell \restriction j: \ell< k\rangle$ is with no
repetition, $\eta_\ell \restriction j = \nu_\ell \restriction j$,
$n(\ell)< n \Rightarrow \eta_\ell = \nu_\ell\}\cup \{\sigma(\ldots,
x^{n(\ell)}_{\eta_\ell}, y_{n(\ell)}, \ldots)<y_n: n(\ell)< n$ and
$\eta_\ell \in {}^\omega 2\}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\label{3.1}
1)  For an $\omega$-sequence $\bar{T} = \langle T_n:n < \omega \rangle$ of
(2,2)-trees, we define a function $\mathrm{degsq}$ (square degree). 

Its domain is $\mathrm{pfap} = \mathrm{pfap}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} = \{(u,g):(\exists n)
(u \in [{}^n 2]^*, g$ is a 2-place function from $u$ to $\omega)\}$
and its values are ordinals (or $\infty$ or $-1$). For this we define the
truth value of ``$\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}(u,g) \ge \alpha"$ by induction on the ordinal
$\alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 1}:  $\alpha=-1$

$\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} (u,g)\ge -1$ \underline{iff} $(u,g) \in \mathrm{pfap}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}$ and
 $\eta,\nu \in u \Rightarrow (\eta,\nu) \in T_{g(\eta,\nu)}$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 2}:  $\alpha$ is limit

$\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}(u,g)\ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} $\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} (u,g) \ge
\beta$ for every $\beta <\alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 3}:  $\alpha = \beta +1$

$\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} (u,g)\ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} for every $\rho^* \in u$, 
for some $m$, $u^* \subseteq {}^m 2,g^*$ and functions $h_0,h_1$, we have:
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $h_i:u \rightarrow u^*$, 

    \item $(\forall \eta \in u) \eta \triangleleft h_i (\eta)$
    
    \item $(\forall\eta \in u) [h_0(\eta) = h_1(\eta) \Leftrightarrow 
    \eta \ne \rho^*]$

    \item $u^* = \mathrm{Rang}(h_0) \cup \mathrm{Rang}(h_1)$

    \item $g^*$ is a 2-place function from $u^*$ to $\omega$ 

    \item $g^*(h_i(\eta),h_i(\nu)) = g(\eta,\nu)$ for $i<2$ and $\eta,\nu\in u$

    \item $\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} (u^*,g^*) \ge \beta$ (so $(u^*,g^*) a\in \mathrm{pfap}$).
\end{enumerate}

\noindent
2)  We define $\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} (u,g) = \alpha$ \underline{iff}
for every ordinal $\beta$, 
$\mathrm{degsq}(u,g) \ge \beta \Leftrightarrow \beta \le \alpha$ 
(so $\alpha = -1$, $\alpha = \infty$ are legal values).

\noindent
3) We define $\mathrm{degsq}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu) =\bigcup\{\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}(u,g)+1: (u,g)\in
\mathrm{pfap}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}\}$.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- claim-12: body_md -->
\label{3.2}
Assume $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$ is an $\omega$-sequence of (2,2)-trees.

\noindent
1)  For every $(u,g) \in \mathrm{pfap}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu},\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu} (u,g)$ is an ordinal,
$\infty$ or $-1$. Any automorphism $F$ of 
$({}^\omega 2,\triangleleft)$ preserves this
(it acts on $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$ too, i.e.  

\[
\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}(u,g) = \mathrm{degsq}_{\langle F(T_n):n<\omega\rangle}(F(u),
g\circ F^{-1}).
\]

\medskip\noindent
2) $\mathrm{degsq}({\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}) =\infty$ \underline{iff} $\mathrm{degsq}({\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}) \ge \omega_1$ 
\underline{iff}  there is a perfect square contained in 
$\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega} \lim (T_n)$ \underline{iff} for some ccc forcing 
notion $\mathbf{P},\Vdash_\mathbf{P} ``\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega} \lim(T_n)$ contains a
$\lambda_{\omega_1}(\aleph_0)$-square" (so those properties are
absolute). 

\noindent
3) If $\mathrm{degsq}({\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu})=\alpha(*)< \omega_1$ \underline{then} 
$\bigcup\limits_{n< \omega} \lim (T_n)$ contains no
$\lambda_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0)$-square.

\noindent
4) For each $\alpha(*)< \omega_1$ there is an $\omega$-sequence 
of $(2,2)$-trees $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu= \langle T_n:n< \omega \rangle$ with 
$\mathrm{degsq}({\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu})=\alpha(*)$.

\noindent
5)  If $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu =\langle T_n:n<\omega\rangle$ is a sequence of
(2,2)-trees \underline{then} the existence of an $\aleph_1$-square in 
$\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega} \lim (T_n)$ is absolute.

\noindent
6)  Moreover for $\alpha(*) < \omega_1$ we have: if
$\mu < \lambda_{\alpha(*)}(\aleph_0)$, $A,B$ disjoint subsets of
${}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$ of cardinality
$\le \mu$, \underline{then} some c.c.c. forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$ adds
$\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu$ as in (4) (i.e. an $\omega$-sequence of $(2,2)$-trees 
$\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu = \langle T_n : n < \omega\rangle$ with 
$\mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu) = \alpha(*)$) such that: 
$A \subseteq \bigcup\limits_{n<\omega} \lim [f(T_n)]$, 
$B \cap \bigcup\limits_{n<\omega} \lim [f(T_n)] = \varnothing$.
<!-- end claim-12 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
asy to prove.  E.g.

\noindent
3) Let $\lambda=\lambda_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0)$ and
assume $\{\eta_i: i<\lambda\} \subseteq {}^\omega 2$, $[i<j\Rightarrow
\eta_i \ne \eta_j]$ and $(\eta_i,\eta_j)\in \bigcup\limits_{n}\lim
(T_n)$ and let $(\eta_i, \eta_j)\in \lim (T_{g(\eta_i, \eta_j)})$.
For $(u,f) \in \mathrm{pfap}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}$, $u=\{\nu_0,\ldots, \nu_{k-1}\}$ (with no
repetition, $<_{lx}$-increasing) let $R_{(u,f)}=\{(\alpha_0,\ldots,
\alpha_{k-1}): \alpha_\ell<\lambda$ and
 $\nu_\ell\triangleleft\eta_{\alpha_\ell}$ for $\ell<k$ and $f(\nu_\ell,
\nu_m)= g(\eta_{\alpha_\ell}, \eta_{\alpha_m})$ for $\ell, m<k\}$.
Let $M=(\lambda, R_{(u,f)})_{(u,f)\in\mathrm{pfap}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}}$.
Clearly if we have $\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_{k-1}<\lambda$ and $n$ such that
$\langle \eta_{\alpha_\ell}\restriction n:
\ell<k\rangle$ is with no repetition, $g(\eta_{\alpha_{\ell(1)}},
\eta_{\alpha_{\ell(2)}})= f(\eta_{\alpha_{\ell(1)}}\restriction n,\ 
\eta_{\alpha_{\ell(2)}}\restriction n)$ then $R_{(u,f)}
(\alpha_0,\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_{k-1})$ and we can then prove

\[
\mathrm{rk}(\{\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_{k-1}\},M) \le \mathrm{degsq}_{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu}(u,f)
\]

\medskip\noindent
(by induction on the left ordinal). But $M$ is a model with countable
vocabulary and cardinality $\lambda=\lambda_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0)$.  Hence
by the definition of $\lambda_{\alpha(*)+1}$ we have $\mathrm{rk}(M) \geq
\alpha(*)+1$, so $\alpha(*)+1 \le \mathrm{rk}(M) \le \mathrm{degsq}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu) \le
\alpha(*)$ (by previous sentence, earlier sentence and a hypothesis
respectively). Contradiction.

\noindent
4) Let $W=\{\eta:\eta$ is a (strictly) decreasing sequence of
ordinals, possibly empty$\}$.

We choose by induction on $i<\omega$, $n_i$ and an
indexed set $\langle (u^i_x, f^i_x, \alpha^i_x): x\in X_i\rangle$ such
that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]   $n_i< \omega$, $n_0=0$, $n_i< n_{i+1}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(b)]  $X_i$ finite including $\bigcup\limits_{j< i}X_j$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(c)]  for $x\in X_i$, $u^i_x \subseteq {}^{n_i}2$, $f^i_x$ a two
place function from $u^i_x$ to $\omega$ and $\alpha^i_x \in w_i$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(d)]   for some $x\in X_i$, $u^i_x =\{0_{n_i}\}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(e)]  $h_i$ is a function from $X_i$ into $W$ and $h_i\subseteq h_{i+1}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(f)]  $|X_0|=1$, and $h_0$ is constantly $\langle\ \rangle$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(g)]  if $x\in X_i$ \underline{then}: $\alpha^{i+1}_x= \alpha^i_x$ and the
function $\nu \mapsto \nu\restriction n_i$ is one to one from
$u^{i+1}_x$ onto $u^i_x$ and $\nu \in u^{i+1}_x \Rightarrow
\nu\restriction [n_i, n_{i+1}) = 0_{[n_i, n_{i+1})}$ and $\eta, \nu \in
u^{i+1}_x \Rightarrow f^{i+1}_x (\eta, \nu) = f^i_x (\eta \restriction
n_i, \nu \restriction n_i)$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(h)]  for some $x=x_i\in X_i$, $\beta = \beta_i \in w_{i+1} \cap
\alpha^i_x$ and $\rho^*=\rho^*_i \in u^i_x$ and $\Upsilon_i \in W$
such that $h_i(x_i)\triangleleft \Upsilon_i$ we can find $y= y_i$
such that:
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(\alpha)$]  $X_{i+1} = X_i \cup \{y_i\}$, $y_i \notin X_i$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\beta)$]  $\alpha^{i+1}_y = \beta$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\gamma)$]  the function $\nu \mapsto \nu \restriction n_i$ is
a function from $u^{i+1}_y$ onto $u^i_x$, almost one to one: $\rho^*$ has
exactly two predecessors say $\rho^y_1$, $\rho^y_2$, and any other
$\rho\in u^i_x \setminus \{\rho^*\}$ has exactly one predecessor in
$u^{i+1}_y$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\delta)$]  for $\nu, \eta\in u^{i+1}_y$ if  $(\nu, \eta)\ne
(\rho^y_1, \rho^y_2)$ and $(\nu, \eta) \ne (\rho^y_2, \rho^y_1)$
then $f^{i+1}_{y_i}(\eta, \nu) = f^i_x (\eta\restriction n_i,
\nu\restriction n_i)$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\varepsilon)$]   $h_{i+1} = h_i \cup \{(y_i, \Upsilon_i)\}$
\end{enumerate}
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(i)]  if $x_1$, $x_2\in X_i$ and $u^i_{x_1}\cap u^i_{x_2}
\neq \varnothing$ {\em then} $u^i_{x_1} \cap u^i_{x_2} = \{ 0_{n_i}\}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(j)]  if $x \in X_i$, $\beta\in w_i \cap \alpha^i_x$, $\rho^*\in
u^i_x$ and $h(x) \triangleleft \Upsilon\in W$, then \, 
for some $j \in (i,\omega)$ we have $x_j = x$,
$\beta_j=\beta$, $\rho^*\trianglelefteq \rho^*_j$ and $\Upsilon_j=
\Upsilon$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(k)] the numbers $f^{i+1}_{y_i}(\rho^{y^1}_1, \rho^{y^1}_2)$,
$f^{i+1}_{y_i}(\rho^{y^2}_2, \rho^{y^2}_1)$ are distinct and do not
belong to $\bigcup\{\mathrm{Rang} (f^i_x):x \in X_i\}$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
There is no problem to carry the definition.

\noindent
We then let

\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{clcr}
T_n = \big\{(\eta,\nu):& \text{for some } i < \omega \text{ and } x \in
X_i \text{ and } \eta',\nu' \in u^i_x \\
  &\text{we have } f^i_x(\eta', \nu')=n \text{ and for some }
\ell \le n_i \text{ we have} \\
  &(\eta,\nu) = (\eta'\restriction \ell, \nu'\restriction \ell) \big\}
\end{array}
\end{equation*}

\medskip\noindent
and $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu = \langle T_n: n< \omega\rangle$. Now it is straight to
compute the rank.

\noindent
5) By the completeness theorem for $L_{\omega_1, \omega}(Q)$ (see
Keisler \cite{Ke71})

\noindent
6) By the proof of \ref{1.10}.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\label{3.3}
Let $T$ be a $(2,2, \kappa)$-tree.  Let $\mathrm{set}(T)$ be the set of all pairs $(u,f)$
such that $$\big(\exists n=n(u,f)\big) \big[ u\subseteq {}^n 2,\ f : u \times
u \rightarrow {}^n \kappa, \text{ and } \eta,\nu \in u \Rightarrow (\eta,\nu, 
f(\eta,\nu))\in T \big].$$
 
We want to define $\mathrm{degsq}_T(x)$ for $x\in\mathrm{set}(T)$. For this we define by
induction on the ordinal $\alpha$ when $\mathrm{degsq}_T(x) \ge \alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 1}:   $\alpha=-1$

$\mathrm{degsq}_T (u,f)\ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} $(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}(T)$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 2}:   $\alpha$ limit

$\mathrm{degsq}_T (u,f) \ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} $\mathrm{degsq}_T (u,f)\ge\beta$ 
for every $\beta < \alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 3}:   $\alpha=\beta +1$

$\mathrm{degsq}_T (u,f)\ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} for every $\eta^* \in u$, for some 
$m>n(u,f)$ there are $(u^*,f^*)\in \mathrm{set}(T)$ and functions $h_0,h_1$
such that $\mathrm{degsq}_T(u^*,f^*) \ge \beta$ and:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]   $n(u^*,f^*)=m$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(ii)]  $h_i$ is a function from $u$ to ${}^m 2$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(iii)]   $\eta \triangleleft h_i (\eta)$ for $i<2$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(iv)]  for $\eta \in u$ we have $h_0(\eta) \ne h_1(\eta)
  \Leftrightarrow \eta =\eta^*$ 
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(v)]   for $\eta_1 \ne \eta_2 \in u,i<2$ we have 
$f(\eta_1,\eta_2) \triangleleft f^* (h_i(\eta_1),h_i(\eta_2))$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(vi)]   for $\eta \in u^*$ we have $f(\eta\restriction n, \; \eta \restriction n)
\triangleleft f^* (\eta, \eta)$
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Lastly, $\mathrm{degsq}_T (u,f) =\alpha$ \underline{iff} for every ordinal $\beta$ we have
$[\beta \le \alpha \Leftrightarrow \mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f) \ge \beta]$.

Also let $\mathrm{degsq} (T)= \mathrm{degsq}_T\big(\big\{\langle\ \rangle \big\},\big\{\big\langle \langle\ \rangle, \langle\ \rangle \big\rangle \big\}\big)$.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- claim-13: body_md -->
\label{3.4}
1) For a $(2,2, \kappa)$-tree $T$, for $(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}(T)$, $\mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f)$ is
an ordinal or infinity or $=-1$. And similarly $\mathrm{degsq} (T)$. All are
absolute. Also $\mathrm{degsq} (T) \ge \kappa^+$ implies $\mathrm{degsq} (T) =\infty$
and similarly for $\mathrm{degsq}_{T}(u, f)$.

\noindent
2)  $\mathrm{degsq} (T)=\infty$ \underline{iff} $\Vdash_\mathbf{P} ``\mathrm{prj}\lim T$
($=\{(\eta,\nu) \in {}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$: for 
some $\rho \in {}^\omega \kappa,
\bigwedge\limits_{n<\omega}(\eta\restriction n, \nu\restriction n, \rho\restriction n)\in T\}$)
contains a perfect square" for every  forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$ including a
trivial one i.e.  $\mathbf{V}^\mathbf{P} = \mathbf{V}$ \underline{iff} 
$\models_\mathbf{P} ``\mathrm{prj}\lim(T)$ contains a $2^{\aleph_0}$-square" 
for the forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$ which is adding $\lambda$ Cohen reals 
for $\lambda=\lambda_{\omega_1} (\kappa)$ some $\lambda$ \underline{iff} 
for some $\mathbf{P},\Vdash_\mathbf{P} ``\mathrm{prj} \lim (T)$ 
contains ?? $\lambda_{\kappa^+} (\aleph_0)$-square".

\noindent
3)  If $\alpha(*) = \mathrm{degsq} (T) < \kappa^+$, then $\mathrm{prj}\lim (T)$ 
does not contain a $\lambda_{\alpha(*)+1}(\kappa)$-square.
<!-- end claim-13 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
) Easy.

\noindent
2) Assume $\mathrm{degsq}(T) = \infty$, and note that $\alpha^* = \{\mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f):
(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}(T)$ and $\mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f) < \infty\} \setminus \{\infty\}$ 
is an ordinal so $(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}(T)$ and $\mathrm{degsq}_{T}(u,f) \ge 
\alpha^* \Rightarrow \mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f) = \infty$
(in fact any ordinal $\alpha \ge \sup\{\mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f)+1:(u,f) \in
\mathrm{set}(T)\}$ will do). Let $\mathrm{set}^\infty(T)= \{(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}(T): 
\mathrm{degsq}_T(u,f) = \infty\}$. 

Now
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)_1$]   there is $(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}^\infty(T)$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(*)_2$]   for every $(u,f) \in \mathrm{set}^\infty(T)$ and $\rho \in
u$ we can find $(u^+,f^+) \in \mathrm{set}^\infty(T)$ and for $e=1,2,h_e: 
u \rightarrow u^+$ such that
$(\forall \eta\in u) (\eta \triangleleft h_e(\eta))$, $(\forall \eta,
\nu \in u) (f(\eta, \nu) \triangleleft f^+(h_e(\eta), h_e(\nu))$,
$(\forall \eta\in u)[h_1(\eta)= h_2(\eta) \Leftrightarrow \eta=\rho]$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
[Why?  As $\mathrm{degsq}_{T}(u, f)=\infty$ it is $\ge \alpha^*+1$ so by the
definition we can find $(u^+, f^+)$, $h_1$, $h_2$ as above but only with
$\mathrm{degsq}_{T}(u^+, f^+) \ge \alpha^*$, but this implies $\mathrm{degsq}_{T}(u^+,
f^+)=\infty$.]
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)_3$]  for every $(u, f) \in \mathrm{set}^\infty(T)$ with
$u=\{\eta_\rho: \rho\in {}^n 2\}\subseteq {}^{(n_1)}2$ (no repetition) we can
find $n_2> n_1$ and $(u^+,f^+) \in \mathrm{set}^\infty(T)$ with 
$u^+ = \{\eta_\rho: \rho \in {}^{n+1}2\}\subseteq {}^{(n_2)}2$ 
(no repetitions) such that
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]   $\rho\in {}^{n+1}2 \Rightarrow \eta_{\rho \restriction n}
\triangleleft \eta_\rho$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(ii)]   for $\rho_1$, $\rho_2\in {}^{n+1} 2$, $\rho_1\restriction n \ne
\rho_2 \restriction n \Rightarrow f(\eta_{\rho_1\restriction n},
\eta_{\rho_2 \restriction n}) \triangleleft f^+(\eta_{\rho_1}, \eta_{\rho_2})$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(iii)]   for $\rho\in {}^{n+1} 2$, 
$f(\eta_{\rho \restriction n}, \eta_{\rho\restriction n}) \triangleleft 
f(\eta_\rho,\eta_\rho)$.  
\end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
[Why?  Repeat $(*)_2$ $2^n$ times.]

So we can find $\langle \eta_\rho: \eta \in {}^n 2\rangle$, $f_n$ by
induction on $n$ such that $\langle \eta_\rho: \rho\in {}^n2\rangle$ is
with no repetition, $\mathrm{degsq}_{T} (\{\eta_\rho: \rho\in {}^n 2\},
f_n)=\infty$, and for each $n$ clauses (i), (ii), (iii) of $(*)_3$ hold, i.e.
 $\rho_1$, $\rho_2 \in {}^{n+1} 2$, $\rho_1 \restriction n \ne
\rho_2\restriction n \Rightarrow f_n(\eta_{\rho_1 \restriction n},
\eta_{\rho_2\restriction n}) \triangleleft f_{n+1} (\eta_{\rho_1},
\eta_{\rho_2})$ and for $\rho\in {}^{n+1}2$ we have
$f_n(\eta_{\rho\restriction n}, \eta_{\rho\restriction n}) \triangleleft
f_{n+1}(\eta_\rho, \eta_\rho)$ and of course 
$\{\eta_\rho: \rho\in {}^m 2\}\subseteq {}^{(k_n)}2$, $k_n< k_{n+1}< \omega$.

So we have proved that the first clause implies the second  
(about the forcing: the $\mathrm{degsq}(T)=\infty$ is absolute so
holds also in $\mathbf{V}^\mathbf{P}$ for any forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$). 
Trivially the second clause implies the third and fourth. 

So assume the third clause and we shall prove the first. By \ref{1.7}
$\lambda^2_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)$ is well defined 
(e.g. $\le \beth_{\kappa^+}$), but 
$\lambda^3_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)= \lambda^2_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)$ by 
\ref{1.4}(3), let $\mathbf{P}$ be the forcing notion adding
$\lambda^2_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)$ Cohen reals. By \ref{1.1}(2) in 
$\mathbf{V}^\mathbf{P}$,
$\lambda_{\kappa^+}(\kappa) \le \lambda^2_{\kappa^+}(\kappa) \le 
2^{\aleph_0}$, and so there are pairwise disjoint 
$\eta_i\in {}^\omega 2$ for $i< \lambda_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)$
such that $(\eta_i, \eta_j) \in \mathrm{prj} \lim(T)$ for $i$, $j<
\lambda_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)$. 

Lastly, we prove forcing implies 
first in $\mathbf{V}^\mathbf{P}$. By part (3) of the claim proved below we
get for every $\alpha< \kappa^+$, as $\lambda_\alpha(\kappa) \le
\lambda_{\kappa^+}(\kappa)$ that $\neg[\alpha = \mathrm{degsq} (T)]$. Hence
$\mathrm{degsq}(T) \ge \kappa^+$, but by part (1) this implies
$\mathrm{degsq}(T)=\infty$.

\noindent
3) Just like the proof of \ref{3.2}(3).
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- claim-14: body_md -->
\label{3.5}
Assume $\alpha(*) < \kappa^+$ and $\lambda <
\lambda_{\alpha(*)}(\kappa)$.

\noindent
1)  For some c.c.c. forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$, in $V^\mathbf{P}$ there is a
$\kappa$-Souslin subset $A = \mathrm{prj}\lim(T)$ (where $T$ is a
$(2,2,\kappa)$-tree) such that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)$]   $A$ contains a $\lambda$-square but $\mathrm{degsq} (T) \le \alpha(*)$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
2)  For given $B \subseteq ({}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2)^\mathbf{V}$
of cardinality $\le \lambda$ we can replace $(*)$ by
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)'$]  $A \cap({}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2)^\mathbf{V} = B$ 
but $\mathrm{degsq}(T) \le \alpha(*)$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end claim-14 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
\label{3.5A}
The following says in fact that ``colouring of pairs is enough": say
for the Hanf number of $L_{\omega_1, \omega}$ below the
continuum, for clarification see \ref{3.6A}.
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- claim-15: body_md -->
\label{3.6}
[MA]  Assume $\lambda< 2^{\aleph_0}$ and $\alpha(*)< \omega_1$ 
is a limit ordinal, $\lambda<\mu := \lambda_{\alpha(*)}(\aleph_0)$. 
\underline{Then} for some symmetric 2-place function $F$ from $\lambda$ to
$\omega$ we have:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)_{\lambda,\mu,F}$]  for no two place (symmetric) function
$F^\prime$ from $\mu$ to $\omega$ do we have:
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(**)$]  for every $n< \omega$, and pairwise distinct $\beta_0,\dots, 
\beta_{n-1} <\mu$ there are pairwise distinct $\alpha_0,\dots,
\alpha_{n-1} <\lambda$ such that
 
\[
\bigwedge\limits_{k< \ell <n}\; F'(\beta_k,\beta_\ell) = 
F(\alpha_k,\alpha_\ell).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end claim-15 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{3.6A}
1)  This is close to Gilchrist Shelah \cite{Sh:491}.

\noindent
2)  The proof of \ref{3.6} says that letting
$R_n = \{(\alpha,\beta):F(\alpha,\beta)=n\}$,
and $N=(\lambda, R_n)_{n<\omega}$, we have $\mathrm{rk}(N)<\alpha(*)$.

\noindent
3) So \ref{3.6} improves \S2 by saying that the examples for the
Hanf number of $L_{\omega_1, \omega}$ below the continuum being large can 
be very simple, speaking only on ``finite patterns" of colouring pairs 
by countably many colours.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
et $M$ be a model of cardinality $\lambda$ with a countable vocabulary
and $\mathrm{rk}^1(M)<\alpha(*)$. Without loss of generality, it has the universe $\lambda$, 
has the relation $<$ and individual constants $c_\alpha$ for $\alpha 
\le \omega$.  Let $k^M$, $\varphi^M$ be as in the proof of \ref{1.10}.

Let $\mathbf{P}$ be the set of triples $(u,f,\mathbf{w})$ such that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]   $u$ is a finite subset of $\lambda$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(b)]  $f$ is a symmetric two place function from $u$ to $\omega$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(c)]   {\bf w} is a family of nonempty subsets of $u$ 
\end{enumerate}
\smallskip\noindent
such that
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(d)]   if $\alpha \in u$ then $\{\alpha\} \in {\mathbf{w}}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(e)]   if $w=\{\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_{n-1}\}\in \mathbf{w}$ (the increasing
enumeration), $k=k^M(w)$, $\alpha\in u\setminus w$ and 
$(\forall \ell)[\ell < n$ and $\ell \ne k \Rightarrow 
f(\alpha_\ell,\alpha) = f(\alpha_\ell,\alpha_k)]$, \underline{then} 
$w\cup\{\alpha\}$ belongs to {\bf w}, $(\forall m \ne k)
(\alpha<\alpha_m \Leftrightarrow \alpha_k<\alpha_m)$ and
$k=k^M(w\cup\{\alpha\} \setminus\{\alpha_k\})$
and $M\models\varphi^M(w)[\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_{k-1},\alpha,
\alpha_{k+1},\ldots,\alpha_{n-1}]$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(f)]   if $w^i=\{\alpha^i_0,\ldots,\alpha^i_{n-1}\}\subseteq u$
(increasing enumeration, so with no repetition), $i=0,1$, and $(\forall
\ell < k<n)[f(\alpha^0_l,\alpha^0_k)=f(\alpha^1_\ell,\alpha^1_k)]$
then $w^0 \in {\bf w} \Leftrightarrow w^1\in {\bf w}$ and if $w^i \in 
\mathbf{w}$ then $\varphi^M(w^0)=\varphi^M(w^1)$, $k^M(w^0)=k^M(w^1)$ and
$\mathrm{rk}^1(w^0,M)= \mathrm{rk}^1(w^1, M)$.
\end{enumerate}
The order is the natural one.

It is easy to check that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\oplus_1$]   $\mathbf{P}$ satisfies the c.c.c.
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$\oplus_2$]   for every $\alpha < \lambda,\mathscr{L}_\alpha=\{(u,f,{\bf w})\in
\mathbf{P}:\alpha\in u\}$ is dense. 
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Hence there is a directed $G \subseteq \mathbf{P}$ not disjoint 
from $\mathscr{L}_\alpha$ for every $\alpha< \lambda$. 
Let $F = \bigcup\{f:$ for some $u$, {\bf w} we have $(u,f,{\bf w}) \in
G\}$.  We shall show that it is as required. Clearly $F$ is a
symmetric two place function from $\lambda$ to $\omega$; so the only thing that can
fail is if there is a symmetric two place function $F'$ from $\mu$
to $\omega$ such that $(**)$ of \ref{3.6} holds.  
By the compactness theorem for propositional logic, there is a 
linear order $<^*$ of $\mu$ such that
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)'$]   for every $n < \omega$ and $\beta_0 <^* \dots <^* 
\beta_{n-1}$ from $\mu$ there are $\alpha_0 < \dots <\alpha_{n-1} 
<\lambda$ such that $\bigwedge_{k< \ell <n} F'(\beta_k,\beta_\ell) =
F(\alpha_k,\alpha_\ell)$. 
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Let
 
\[
\mathbf{W}= \bigcup\{\mathbf{w}:\text{ for some } u,f \text{ we have }
(u,f,\mathbf{w}) \in G\}.
\]

\medskip\noindent
Let $N = (\lambda,R_n)_{n< \omega}$ where $R_n=\{(\alpha,\beta):
F(\alpha,\beta)=n\}$, so $\mathrm{rk}^1(w,N)$ for $w\in [\lambda]^*$
is well defined; in fact we can restrict ourselves to formulas of the form
$\varphi(x_0,\ldots,x_{n-1}) =
\bigwedge\limits_{\ell<k<n}F(x_\ell,x_k)=c_{m(\ell,k)}$. Let
$N'=(\mu,R_n')_{n< \omega}$, where $R_n'=\{(\alpha,\beta):
F'(\alpha,\beta)=n\}$. 

Now first note that
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes_1$]   If $w \in \mathbf{W} \text{ then } 
\mathrm{rk}^1(w,N) \le \mathrm{rk}^1(w,M)$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
(This is the role of clause (e) in the definition of $\mathbf{P}$; we prove it by
induction on $\mathrm{rk}^1(w,M)$ using the same ``witness" $k^M$.)  

Secondly,
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes_2$]  For $\alpha < \lambda$ we have $\{\alpha\} \in \mathbf{W}$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
(This is the role of clause (d) in the definition of $\mathbf{P}$.)
Hence we conclude (by \ref{1.1}(2), $\otimes_1$, $\otimes_2$):
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes_3$]  $\mathrm{rk}^1(N) < \alpha(*)$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Lastly
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes_4$]  \underline{If} $\alpha_0 <^* \ldots <^* \alpha_{m-1} < \mu$, $\beta_0 < \ldots < \beta_{m-1} <\lambda$,
$\bigwedge\limits_{k < \ell<m} F'(\alpha_k,\alpha_l) = F(\beta_k,\beta_l)$ and
$\{\beta_0,\ldots,\beta_{m-1}\} \in \mathbf{W}$, \underline{then} 
$$\mathrm{rk}^1(\{\alpha_0,\ldots,\alpha_{m-1}\},N') \le \mathrm{rk}^1(\{\beta_0,
\ldots,\beta_{m-1}\},N)$$
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
(again it can be proved by induction on
$\mathrm{rk}^1(\{\beta_\ell,\ldots,\beta_{m-1}\},N)$, the choice of $N'$ and our
assumption toward contradiction that $(**)$ from the claim holds).
Now by $\otimes_3$, $\otimes_4$ and \ref{1.1}(2) (and $\otimes_2$) we have
$\mathrm{rk}^1(N') \le \mathrm{rk}^1(N)<\alpha(*)$ but this contradicts
$\|N'\|=\mu=\lambda_{\alpha(*)}(\aleph_0)$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- claim-16: body_md -->
\label{3.7}
[MA]  If $\lambda,\mu,F$ are as in \ref{3.6} (i.e. $(*)_{\lambda,\mu,F}$ holds)
\underline{then} some Borel set $B \subseteq {}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$  
(actually of the form
$\bigcup\limits_{n< \omega} \lim(T_n))$ has a $\lambda$-square but no
$\mu$-square.
<!-- end claim-16 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
\label{3.7A}
1) The converse holds too, of course.

\noindent
2)  Instead of $\lambda$ we can use ``all $\lambda < \mu"$.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
ithout loss of generality, $\mathrm{cf}(\lambda)>\aleph_0$ (otherwise combine $\omega$ examples).
                              
Let $F$ be a symmetric two place function from $\lambda$ to
$\omega$ such that $(*)_{\lambda,\mu,F}$. 
For simplicity let $f^*:\omega \rightarrow \omega$ be such that
$\forall n \exists^\infty m f^* (m) =n$. We define a forcing notion
$\mathbf{P}$ as in \ref{1.10} except that we require in addition for $p
\in \mathbf{P}$:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\otimes_1$]  $f^*(g^p (\alpha,\beta)) = F(\alpha,\beta)$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$\otimes_2$]   if $\alpha' \ne \beta',\alpha'' \ne \beta''$ 
are from $u^p,k<\omega,\eta_{\alpha'}^p \restriction k=
\eta_{\alpha''}^p \restriction k \ne \eta_{\beta'}^p 
\restriction k=\eta_{\beta''}^p \restriction k$ both not constantly 1 then
$F(\alpha',\beta') = F(\alpha'',\beta'')$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$\otimes_3$]  if $\eta,\nu\in {}^{n[p]}2$ then for at most one $m<m[p]$
we have $(\eta,\nu)\in t^p_m$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$\otimes_4$]  \underline{iff} $n< \omega$, $n>1,\;\eta_0<_{lx} \dots
<_{lx} \eta_{n-1}$ are pairwise distinct members of ${}^{n(p)}2$ 
and $k < \ell \Rightarrow (\eta_k,\eta_\ell) \in
t^p_{g(k,\ell)}$,\underline{then} for some pairwise distinct $\alpha_0,\dots, 
\alpha_{n-1}$ from $u[p^n]$, we have 
$\bigwedge\limits_{k< \ell <n} f^*(h(k,\ell))= F(\alpha_k,\alpha_\ell)$. 
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
We have
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\oplus_1$]  if $\alpha< \beta <\lambda$, there is a unique $n <
  \omega$ such that $(\let\theaccent\tilde\skewfactor-
\mathpalette\oalign{$3\eta$\crcr\hidewidth
\vbox to.2ex{\hbox{$3\skew\skewfactor\theaccent{}$}\vss}\hidewidth}_\alpha,\let\theaccent\tilde\skewfactor-
\mathpalette\oalign{$3\eta$\crcr\hidewidth
\vbox to.2ex{\hbox{$3\skew\skewfactor\theaccent{}$}\vss}\hidewidth}_\beta) 
\in \lim \let\theaccent\widetilde\skewfactor-
\mathpalette\oalign{$3T$\crcr\hidewidth
\vbox to.2ex{\hbox{$3\skew\skewfactor\theaccent{}$}\vss}\hidewidth}_n$. 
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Thus $\bigcup\limits_{n\in\omega} T_n$ contains a 
$\lambda$-square. In proving that it does not contain a $\mu$-square 
we apply $(*)_{\lambda,\mu,F}$. For this the crucial fact is:
\medskip\noindent 
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\oplus_2$]   if $n< \omega$, $\eta_0,\dots,\eta_{n-1} \in
  {}^\omega 2$ are distinct, $(\eta_k,\eta_\ell) \in \lim
  (T_{h(k,\ell)})$, \underline{then} for some pairwise distinct 
$\alpha_0,\dots,\alpha_{n-1} < \lambda$

\[
\bigwedge\limits_{k<\ell<n}f^*(h(k,\ell))=F(\alpha_k,\alpha_\ell).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Instead of ``$\mathcal{I}^3_\alpha$ is dense" it is enough to show
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$\oplus_3$]  for some $p \in \mathbf{P}$, $p \Vdash$ 
``the number of $\alpha<\lambda$ such that for some $q \in 
\let\theaccent\widetilde\skewfactor-
\mathpalette\oalign{$3G$\crcr\hidewidth
\vbox to.2ex{\hbox{$3\skew\skewfactor\theaccent{}$}\vss}\hidewidth}_\mathbf{P}$ $\alpha \in u^q$ is $\lambda"$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- claim-17: body_md -->
\label{3.8}
Assume $\aleph_0 < \lambda < 2^{\aleph_0}$. Then the following are
equivalent:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]  For some symmetric 2-place functions 
$F_\mu$ from $\mu$ to $\omega$ (for $\mu < \lambda$) we have 
\smallskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)_{\langle F_\mu:\mu<\lambda\rangle}$]  for no two place function
$F'$ from $\lambda$ to $\omega$ do we have: 
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(**)$]  for every $n< \omega$ and pairwise distinct 
$\beta_0,\dots,\beta_{n-1} < \lambda$ there are $\mu < \lambda$ and 
pairwise distinct $\alpha_0,\dots,\alpha_{n-1} < \mu$ such that

\[
\bigwedge\limits_{k< \ell<n} F'(\beta_k,\beta_\ell) = 
F_\mu(\alpha_k,\alpha_\ell).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(b)]  Some Borel subset of ${}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$ 
contains a $\mu$-square \underline{iff} $\mu< \lambda$ (in fact $B$ is an 
$F_\sigma$ set).
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end claim-17 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\label{4.1}
We shall define $$\mathrm{Prk}^\ell_\alpha (\lambda_1,\lambda_2;< \kappa,
\theta_0,\theta_1),\mathrm{rkk}^\ell((w_1,w_2),M,\lambda_1,\lambda_2;\kappa,
\theta_0,\theta_1)$$ as in \ref{1.1} (but $w_\ell \in [R^M_\ell]^*$ 
and $|R^M_\ell| = \lambda_\ell$), replacing $\mathrm{rk}$ by $\mathrm{rkk}$ etc. Let
$\bar{\lambda}=(\lambda_1,\lambda_2)$, $\bar w=(w_1,w_2)$.

\noindent
1)  For $\ell < 6$, and cardinals $\bar\lambda=(\lambda_1,\lambda_2)$,
$\lambda_1,\lambda_2 \ge \kappa$ and $\bar\theta=(\theta_0,\theta_1)$,
$\theta_0 \le \theta_1 <\lambda_1,\lambda_2$ and an ordinal $\alpha$ let
$\mathrm{Prk}^\ell_\alpha (\bar\lambda;<\kappa,\bar\theta)$ mean that: 
for every model $M$ with vocabulary of cardinality $\le\theta_0$, such that
$\bigwedge\limits_{i=1}^{2} |R^M_i|=\lambda_i$, $R^M_1\cap R^M_2 =\varnothing$,
$F^M$ is a two place function with range included in $\theta_1=Q^M$,
we have $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(M;< \kappa) \ge \alpha$ (defined below).  

Let $\mathrm{NPrk}^\ell_\alpha (\bar\lambda; <\kappa,\bar\theta)$ be the
negation.  Instead of $<\kappa^+$ we
may write $\kappa$; if $\kappa=\theta^+_0$ we may omit $\theta_0$; if
$\theta_0=\aleph_0, \;\kappa=\aleph_1$, we may omit them. We may write
$\theta_0$, $\theta_1$ instead $\bar{\theta}=(\theta_0,\theta_1)$ 
and similarly for $\bar\lambda$. 

Lastly, we let $\lambda \mathrm{rc}^\ell_\alpha(\kappa,\bar\theta)=
\min\{\lambda:\mathrm{Prk}^\ell_\alpha(\lambda,\lambda;<\kappa,\bar
\theta)\}$. 

\noindent
2)  For a model $M$, $\mathrm{rkrc}^\ell(M;<\kappa) = 
\sup\{\mathrm{rkrc}^\ell(\bar w,M;<\kappa)+1 : \bar w = \langle w_1, w_2\rangle$
 where $w_i \subseteq R_i^M$  are finite non empty,
$(\exists c \in Q^M)(\forall (a,b) \in w_1 \times w_2)[F(a,b)=c]\}$ where $\mathrm{rkk}$ is as defined in part (3) below.

\noindent
3) For a model $M$, and $$\bar w \in [M]^\otimes := 
\{(u_1,u_2) : u_i\subseteq R^M_i \text{ finite nonempty, }
(\exists c)(\forall a\in u_1, b \in u_2)[F(a,b) = c]\}$$
we shall define the truth value of $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M;<\kappa)
\ge \alpha$ by induction on the ordinal $\alpha$ (for
$\ell=0,1$, $\kappa$ can be omitted).
If we write $w$ instead of $w_1,w_2$ we mean $w_1 = w\cap R^M_1$, 
$w_2 = w\cap R^M_2$ (here $R^M_1 \cap R^M_2 =\varnothing$ helps).

Then we can note:
\medskip\noindent 
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)_0$]   $\alpha \le \beta$ and $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M;<\kappa)
  \ge \beta \Rightarrow \mathrm{rkk}^\ell (\bar w,M;<\kappa) \ge \alpha$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(*)_1$]  $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M;<\kappa) \ge \delta$ ($\delta$ limit)
\underline{iff} $\bigwedge\limits_{\alpha< \delta} \mathrm{rkk}^\ell (\bar w,M;<
\kappa) \ge \alpha$ 
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(*)_2$]  $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M;<\kappa) \ge 0$ \underline{iff} 
$\bar w \in [M]^\otimes$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
So we can define $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M ;<\kappa)=\alpha$ for the maximal $\alpha$
such that $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M ;<\kappa)\ge \alpha$, and $\infty$ if this holds
for every $\alpha$ (and $-1$ if $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M ;<\kappa) \ngeq 0$).
Now the inductive definition of $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M ;<\kappa) \ge \alpha$ was
already done above for $\alpha=0$ and $\alpha$ limit, so for 
$\alpha = \beta +1$ we let 
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)_3$]  $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M ;<\kappa) \ge \beta +1$ \underline{iff} (letting
$w=w_1 \cup w_2$, $n=|w|$, $w=\{a_0,\dots,a_{n-1}\}$), we have: for every
$k<n$ and quantifier free formula $\varphi(x_0,\dots,x_{n-1})= 
\bigwedge\limits_{i<j} x_i \ne x_j$ and 
$\bigwedge\{R_1(x_i)\ \wedge\ R_2(x_j)\ \wedge\
\varphi_{i,j}(x_i,x_j): R_1(a_i)$ and $R_2(a_j)\}$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent 
(In the vocabulary of $M$) for which $M \models 
\varphi[a_0,\dots,a_{n-1}]$ we have:
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 1} :   $\ell=1$.

There are $a^i_m \in M$ for $m<n$, $i<2$ such that:
\medskip\noindent 
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]  $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\{a^i_m:i<2,m< n\},M; <\kappa) \ge \beta$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(b)]  $M \models \varphi[a^i_0,\dots,a^i_{n-1}]$ (for $i=1,2$), so 
there is no repetition in $a^i_0,\dots,a^i_{n-1}$ and $[a^i_m\in
R^M_j\ \Leftrightarrow\ a_m\in R^M_j]$ for $j=1,2$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(c)]   $a^0_k \ne a^1_k$ but if $m<n$ and $(a_m\in R^M_1\
\Leftrightarrow\ a_k\notin R^M_1)$ then $a^0_m=a^1_m$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(d)]  if $a_{m_1} \in R^M_1$, $a_{m_2}\in R^M_2$ then for any $i,j$
($\in \{1, 2\}$) we have $F^M(a^i_{m_1},a^j_{m_2})=F^M(a_{m_1},a_{m_2})$. 
\end{enumerate}
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 2}:  $\ell=0$

As for $l=1$ but in addition
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate} 
\item[(e)]  $\bigwedge\limits_{m} a_m=a^0_m$.
\end{enumerate}
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 3}:  $\ell=3$

The definition is like case 1 but $i<\kappa$; i.e. there are 
$a^i_m \in M$ for $m<n$, $i< \kappa$ such that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)]  for $i<j<\kappa$ we have 
$\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\{a^i_m,a^j_m: m<n\}, M;<\kappa) \ge \beta$
\smallskip\noindent 
\item[(b)]  $M \models \varphi[a^i_0,\dots,a^i_{n-1}]$ (for $i<\kappa$; so  
there are no repetitions in $a^i_0,\dots,a^i_{n-1}$)
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(c)]  for $i < j < \kappa$, $a^i_k \ne a^j_k$ but if  $m < n$ and 
$(a_m \in R^M_1 \Leftrightarrow a_k \notin R^M_1)$ then $a^i_m = a^j_m$  
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(d)]  if $a_{m_1}\in R^M_1$, $a_{m_2}\in R^M_2$ then for any $i,j$,
$F^M(a^i_{m_1},a^j_{m_2}) = F^M(a_{m_1},a_{m_2})$. 
\end{enumerate}
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 4}:  $\ell=2$

Like case 3 but in addition:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(e)] $a_m=a^0_m$ for $m<n$.
\end{enumerate}
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 5}:  $\ell=5$

Like case 3 except that we replace clause (a) by:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(a)$^-$]  for every function $H$, $\mathrm{Dom}(H)=\kappa$, $|\mathrm{Rang}(H)|<\kappa$
for some $i<j<\kappa$ we have $H(i)=H(j)$ and
$\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\{a^i_m,a^j_m:m<n\},M;<\kappa) \ge \beta$.
\end{enumerate}
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 6}:  $\ell=4$

Like case 4 using clause (a)$^-$ instead (a).

\noindent
4)  For $M$ as above and $c\in Q^M$ we define $\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(M,c; <\kappa)$ as
$$\sup \big\{\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(\bar w,M;< \kappa)+1: \bar w \in [M]^\otimes,\ 
(\forall a \in w_1,\forall b \in w_2)[F(a,b)=c]\big\}.$$

\noindent
5) Let $\mathrm{Prrd}^\ell_\alpha(\bar \lambda, \kappa, \bar \theta)$ mean
$\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(M, c; <\kappa, \bar \theta) \ge \alpha$ for every $M$ 
for some $c\in M$ when $M$ is such that
$|R^M_1| =\lambda_1$, $|R^M_2|=\lambda_2$, $|\tau(M)| \le \theta_0$,
$F^M: R^M_1 \times R^M_2 \rightarrow Q^M$, $|Q^M|=\theta_1$. Let
$\mathrm{NPrrd}^\ell_\alpha(\bar \lambda, \kappa, \bar \theta)$ mean its
negation and $\lambda \mathrm{rd}^\ell_\alpha(\kappa,\bar \theta)$ be the
minimal $\lambda$ such that $\mathrm{Prrd}^\ell_\alpha(\lambda,\lambda, \kappa,
\bar \theta)$.
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
\label{4.1A}
The reader may wonder why in addition to $\mathrm{Prk}$ we use the variant $\mathrm{Prrd}$. 
The point is that for the existence of the rectangle $X_1 \times X_2$ with
$F \restriction (X_1 \times X_2)$ constantly $c^*$, this constant plays
a special role. 
So in our main claim \ref{4.4}, to get a model as there, we need to 
choose it, one out of $\theta_1$, but the other choices are out of $\kappa$. So
though the difference between the two variants is small (see \ref{4.3A} below)
we actually prefer the $\mathrm{Prrd}$ version.
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- claim-18: body_md -->
\label{4.2}
The parallels of \ref{1.2} (+statements in \ref{1.1}), also
    \ref{1.3},                                             
\ref{1.4}(2), \ref{1.5}, \ref{1.8} hold.
<!-- end claim-18 -->
<!-- claim-19: body_md -->
\label{4.3}
1) If $w_i\in [R_i^M]^*$ for $i=1,2$ then 
$\omega \times \mathrm{rkk}^l(\langle w_1,w_2 \rangle,M;\kappa) \ge 
\mathrm{rk}^l(w_1\cup w_2,M;\kappa)$.

\noindent
2) If $R^M_1=R^M_2$ (abuse of the notation) then 
$\mathrm{rkk}^\ell(M;<\kappa) \ge \mathrm{rk}^\ell(M;\kappa)$.

\noindent
3) If $\lambda_1=\lambda_2=\lambda$ then $\Pr_\alpha(\lambda,\kappa) 
\Rightarrow \mathrm{Prk}_\alpha(\lambda_1,\lambda_2;\kappa,\kappa)$.
<!-- end claim-19 -->
<!-- claim-20: body_md -->
\label{4.3A}
$\lambda \mathrm{rd}^\ell_\alpha(\kappa,\theta) = \lambda \mathrm{rc}^\ell_\alpha(\kappa,
\theta)$ \underline{iff} $\alpha$ is a successor ordinal or $\mathrm{cf}\!(\alpha) >
\theta$.
<!-- end claim-20 -->
<!-- claim-21: body_md -->
\label{4.4}
Assume $\kappa \le \theta< \lambda_1,\lambda_2$. \underline{Then} the
following are equivalent:

\noindent
1) $\mathrm{Prrd}_{\kappa^+}^1 (\lambda_1,\lambda_2;\kappa,\theta)$.

\noindent
2) Assume $M$ is a model with a countable vocabulary, $|R^M_\ell|=\lambda_\ell$
for $\ell=1,2,\quad P^M=\kappa, Q^M=\theta$, and $F^M$ a two-place function
(really just $F\restriction (R^M_1\times R^M_2)$ interests us) and the range of
$F\restriction (R^M_1\times R^M_2)$ is included in $Q^M$ and $G$ is a
function from $[R_1^M]^*\times[R_2^M]^*$ to $P^M$. \underline{Then} we
can find $\tau (M)$-models $M_0, N$ and elements, $c^*,a_\eta,b_\eta$ 
(for $\eta \in {}^\omega 2$) such that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(i)]   $N$ is a model with the vocabulary of $M$ (but functions may be
interpreted as partial ones, i.e. as relations)
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(ii)]  $a_\eta \in R^N_1,\;b_\eta \in R^N_2$ are pairwise distinct 
and $F^{N} (a_\eta,\;b_\eta)=c^*(\in N)$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(iii)]  $M_0$ countable, $M_0\subseteq M, \quad c^*\in Q^M$, $M_0$
is the closure of $(M_0\cap P^M) \cup \{c^*\} $ in $M$, in fact for some
$M'_0 \prec M$ we have $M_0=$ closure of $P^{M'_0}\cup \{c^*\}$, $c^*\in
M'_0$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(iv)]  $M_0 \subseteq N$, $P^{M_0}=P^N$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(v)]  $|N|=\{\sigma(a_\eta,b_\nu,\bar d):\sigma$ is a 
$\tau(M)$-term, $\eta \in {}^\omega 2,\nu \in {}^\omega 2$ and 
$\bar d\subseteq M_0\}$ 
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(vi)]  for $\{\eta_\ell:\ell < \ell(*)\},\{\nu_m: m<m(*)\}
\subseteq {}^\omega 2$ (both without repetitions non-empty) 
there is $d^1 \in P^{M_0}$ such that if $\bar d\subseteq
P^{M_0}$ and quantifier free formulas $\varphi_{l,m}$ are such that
$N \models \bigwedge\limits_{\ell< \ell(*),m<m(*)} 
\varphi_{\ell,m}[a_{\eta_\ell},b_{\nu_m},\bar d]$, \underline{then} 
for some $\{a_\ell: \ell< \ell(*)\} \subseteq R^M_1$,
$\{b_m:m<m(*)\} \subseteq R^M_2$ (both with no repetition) we have
$M \models \bigwedge\limits_{\ell< \ell(*),m<m(*)} 
\varphi_{\ell,m}[a_\ell,b_m,\bar d]$ and 
$G(\{a_l:\ell < \ell(*)\},\{b_m:m<m(*)\})=d^1$
\smallskip\noindent
\item[(vii)]  for every quantifier free first order $\varphi
= \varphi(x, y, z_0,\ldots)\in L(\tau(M))$ and $d_0,\ldots\in M_0$ 
there is $k< \omega$ such that: for every $\eta_1$, $\eta_2$, 
$\nu_1$, $\nu_2\in {}^\omega 2$ such that 
$\eta_1 \restriction k= \eta_2\restriction k$,
$\nu_1\restriction k = \nu_2\restriction k$ we have
\[
N \models \varphi[a_{\eta_1}, b_{\nu_1}, d_1, \ldots] = 
\varphi[a_{\eta_2}, b_{\nu_2}, d_1, \ldots].
\]
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
Moreover
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[(vii)$^+$]  for every $n< \omega$, first order $\varphi  =
\varphi(x_0,\dots,x_{n-1})\in L(\tau (M))$ quantifier free 
and $d_2,d_3,\ldots\in M_0$ \underline{there is} $n^*<\omega$ such that: 
for every $k\in(n^*,\omega)$, $\eta_0, \eta_1 \in {}^\omega 2,\nu_0,\nu_1
\in {}^\omega 2$ satisfying $\eta_0\restriction k= \eta_1 \restriction k$ and 
$\nu_0\restriction k= \nu_1 \restriction k$ we have 
$N \models \varphi[a_{\eta_0},b_{\nu_0}, d_2,\dots]\equiv \varphi [a_{\eta_1},
b_{\nu_1}, d_2,\dots,]$
\smallskip\noindent 
\item[(viii)]  if $\varphi$ is an existential sentence in $\tau(M)$ satisfied
by $N$ \underline{then} $\varphi$ is satisfied by $M$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
3) Like 2 omitting (vii)$^+$, (viii).
<!-- end claim-21 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
{$(B)^- \Rightarrow (A)$}:

Toward contradiction assume $\mathrm{NPrrd}_{\kappa^+}(\lambda_1, \lambda_2;
\kappa)$ hence there is a model $M'$ witnessing it, so
$|\tau(M')| \le \kappa$. So $c \in Q^M \Rightarrow \mathrm{rkk}^1(M', c;
\kappa) < \kappa^+$ (note that $\mathrm{Prrd}$ was defined by cases of   
$\mathrm{rkk}(M, c, \kappa)$).

Let $\{\varphi_i(x,y):i<\kappa\}$ list the quantifier free 
formulas in $L_{\omega, \omega}(\tau(M'))$ with free variables $x,y$.
Let $\{u_i:i<\kappa\}$ list the finite subsets of $\kappa$. For 
$c\in Q^{M'}$ and $a_0,\ldots,a_{\ell(*)-1}\in R^{M'}_1,
b_0,\ldots,b_{m(*)-1}\in R^{M'}_2$
($\bar{a}=\langle a_0,\ldots,a_{\ell(*)-1}\rangle$, $\bar{b}=\langle
b_0,\ldots,b_{m(*)-1}\rangle$ and for notation let $a_{n+1+\ell}=b_\ell$) let

\[
\alpha_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}}=\mathrm{rkk}^1((\{a_0,\ldots,a_{\ell(*)-1}\},
\{b_0,\ldots,b_{m(*)-1}\}), M', c; \kappa),
\]

\medskip\noindent 
and $k_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}},\varphi_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}}$ be witnesses 
for $\mathrm{rkk}^1((\bar{a},\bar{b}),M',c;\kappa,\theta) \ngeq
\alpha_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}}+1$.  Let $i(c,\bar{a},\bar{b})<\kappa$ 
be such that $\varphi_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}}$ is a
conjunction of formulas of the form $\varphi_j(x_\ell,y_m)$ for $j\in
u_{i(c,\bar{a},\bar{b})}$. 

We define $M$:

the universe is $|M'|$,

\noindent 
the function $F^{M'}$, relations $R^{M'}_1,R^{M'}_2,Q^{M'},P^{M'}$, the pairing
function on ordinals, 

\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{clcr}
R_n = \{(i,a,b):&a \in R^M_1, b\in R^M_2 \mbox{ and if } 
|u_i|>n \text{ then} \\
  &M \models \varphi_j[a,b] \text{ where } j \text{ is the n-th member
    of } u_i\}
\end{array}
\end{equation*}

\medskip\noindent
and let $H_c$ be one to one from $\omega \times \mathrm{rkk}(M',c; \kappa,
\theta)\times \kappa$ into $\kappa$;\quad we define the function $G:
G_c(\bar{a},\bar{b})= H(G_{c,0}(\bar{a},\bar{b}), G_{c,1}(\bar{a},\bar{b}),
G_{c,2}(\bar a, \bar b)) = H(k_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}},\alpha_{c,\bar{a},\bar{b}},
i_{c,\bar a, \bar b})$.

Now we can apply statement (B)-of \ref{4.4} which we are assuming and get
$M_0, N, c^*$, $a_\eta, b_\eta$
(for $\eta \in {}^\omega 2$) satisfying clauses (i)---(vii) there.
So $c^*\in M_0\subseteq M'\cap N$, so $\beta^*= \mathrm{rkk}(M',c ; \kappa)$ 
satisfies $\beta^*<\infty$, even $< \kappa^+$. Clearly 
$\mathrm{rkk}^1(N, c^*; \kappa) \le \beta^*$.

Consider all sequences
$\langle \langle \eta_\ell: \ell< \ell(*)\rangle,
\langle \nu_m: m< m(*)\rangle,d_1,\bar d,\langle \varphi_{\ell, m}:
\ell< \ell(*), m< m(*)\rangle,\langle a_\ell: \ell< \ell(*)\rangle,
\langle b_m: m< m(*)\rangle \big\rangle$ which are as in clause (vi) of (B).

Among those tuples choose one with $\alpha^* = \mathrm{rkk}^1(\{a_\ell: \ell<
\ell(*)\}, \{b_m: m< m(*)\}, N, c^*; \kappa)$ minimal. Let this
rank not being $\ge \alpha^*+1$ be exemplified by $\varphi$ and $k<
\ell(*)+ m(*)$, so by symmetry without loss of generality $k< \ell(*)$.

Choose $k^*< \omega$ large enough for clause $(vi)$ of (B) for 
all formulas $\varphi(x, y)$ appearing in $\{\varphi_j(x, y):j
\in u_{i_{c^*, \bar a, \bar b}}\}$ where 
$i_{c^*, \bar a, \bar b}= G_{c^*, 2}(\bar a, \bar b)$ and 
$\langle \eta_\ell \restriction k^*: \ell < \ell(*)\rangle,
\langle \nu_m \restriction k^*: m< m(*)\rangle$ and
with no repetition. Choose $\eta_{\ell(*)}\in 2^\omega \setminus
\{\eta_k\}$ such that $\eta_{\ell(*)} \restriction k^* = \eta_k
\restriction k^*$. Now apply clause (vi) of (B)-to $\bar
\eta'=\langle \eta_\ell: \ell \le \ell(*)\rangle$, $\bar \nu'=\langle
\nu_m: m< m(*)\rangle$, $\bigwedge\limits_{m} \varphi_{\ell(*),
m}(x_{\ell(*)}, x_m),d'_1, \bar d'$.

By the choice of $\varphi$, these clearly satisfy $\mathrm{rkk}^1(\{a'_\ell: \ell<
\ell(*)\},\{b'_m:m< m(*)\}, N, c^*;\kappa, \bar \theta)< \mathrm{rkk}^1(\{a_\ell:
\ell< \ell(*)\}, \{b_m: m< m(*)\}, N, c^*; \kappa, \bar \theta) = \alpha^*$,
but by this we easily contradict the choice of $\alpha^*$ as minimal.

(A) $\Rightarrow$ (B)

As in the proof of \ref{2.4}, \ref{2.1} (choosing a fixed $c$).

(B) $\Rightarrow$ (B)$^-$

Trivial.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- discussion-1: body_md -->
\label{4.5}
1) When applying \ref{4.4} (1) $\Rightarrow$ (2), or \ref{2.4} we
can use $M$ which is an expansion of $(\mathscr{H}(\chi),\;\in, <^*)$ 
by Skolem functions, $P^M=\kappa$, $\chi$
large enough, so for $\eta,\nu\in {}^\omega 2,N_{\eta,\nu} :=
c \ell_N(M_0\cup \{a_\eta, a_\nu\})$ is a model of ZFC, not 
well founded but with standard $\omega$ and more: its 
$\{i: i< \kappa\}$ is a part of the true $\kappa$.
In \cite{Sh:532} we will have as in \ref{2.1} $M_0\subseteq N$,
$M_0\prec M, \; M_0\prec N_{\eta,\nu}$, and if
$N_{\eta,\nu}\models``\varphi(\eta,\nu),\nu,\eta$ are (essentially) in
${}^\omega 2,\varphi \in M_0$ a $\kappa$-Souslin relation'', then 
$\mathbf{V} \models \varphi(\eta,\nu)$. 

\noindent
2) We can give a rank to subsets of $\lambda_1\times\lambda_2$ and have
parallel theorems.
<!-- end discussion-1 -->
<!-- claim-22: body_md -->
\label{4.6}
If $\varphi \subseteq {}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$ is $\bigvee\limits_{i
<\theta} \varphi_i$, each $\varphi_i$ is $\kappa$-Souslin, $\varphi$  
contains a $(\lambda_1,\lambda_2)$-rectangle, and 
$\mathrm{Prrd}_{\kappa^+ +1}^1 (\lambda_1,\lambda_2;\theta)$, \underline{then}
$\varphi$ contains a perfect rectangle.
<!-- end claim-22 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
et $\varphi_i(\eta, \nu) = (\exists \rho) [(\eta, \nu, \rho)\in
\lim(T_i)]$ where $T_i$ is a $(2,2, \kappa)$-tree.
Let $M$ be $({\mathscr{H}}(\chi),\in, <^*_\chi, \langle T_i: i<
\theta\rangle, h, \Upsilon, \lambda_1, \lambda_2, R_1, R_2, Q, n)_{n<
\omega}$ expanded by Skolem functions, where $Q^M= \theta$ and choosing
$\eta_i\in {}^\omega 2$ for $i< \lambda_1$ pairwise distinct,
$\nu_j \in {}^\omega 2$ for $j< \lambda_2$ are pairwise distinct,
$R^M_1=\{\eta_i: i< \lambda_1\}$, $R^M_2=\{\nu_j: j< \lambda_2\}$ and
let $\Upsilon$, $h$ be functions such that $(\eta_i, \nu_j,
\Upsilon(\eta_i, \nu_j))\in \lim (T_{h(\eta_i, \nu_j)})$.
So let $N$, $M_0$, $c^*$, $a_\eta$ (for $\eta\in {}^\omega 2$), $b_\eta$
(for $\eta\in {}^\omega 2$) be as in clause (B) of \ref{4.4}. Now $M$
has elimination of quantifiers, so there are quantifier free formulas
$\varphi^\ell_n(x)$ saying (in $M$) that $x\in R_\ell\ \&\ x(n)=1$, and
$H_n(x, y)$ be such that $x\in R^M_1\ \&\ y\in R^M_2 \Rightarrow
(\Upsilon(x, y))(n) = H_n(x, y)\in \kappa= P^M$.

So for $\eta\in {}^\omega 2$ we can define $\sigma^1_{\eta} \in
{}^\omega 2$ by $\sigma^1_\eta(n)=1 \Leftrightarrow N\models
\varphi^1_n(a_\eta)$ and $\sigma^1_\eta \in 
{}^\omega 2$ by $\sigma^2_\eta (n)=1
\Leftrightarrow N\models \varphi^2_n(b_\eta)$ and we define, for $\eta$,
$\nu\in {}^\omega 2$, a sequence $\sigma_{\eta, \nu} \in {}^{\omega
>}(P^{M_0})\subseteq {}^{\omega >} \kappa$ by $\sigma_{\eta, \nu (n)} =
H_n(a_\eta,b_\nu)$.

Now $A=:\{\sigma^1_\eta: \eta\in {}^\omega 2\}$,
$B=:\{\sigma^2_\eta: \eta\in {}^\omega 2\}$ are perfect and for $\eta$,
$\nu\in {}^\omega 2$, $(\sigma^1_\eta, \sigma^2_\eta, \sigma_{\eta,
\nu})\in \lim (T)$ hence $A\times B$ is a perfect rectangle inside $\mathrm{prj}
\lim (T)$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- fact-3: body_md -->
\label{4.7}
1)  Assume that $\varphi \subseteq {}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$ 
is $\theta_1$-Souslin, $\kappa< \theta_1$,\quad $\theta=
\mathrm{cf}\!\left(S_{\le\kappa}(\theta_1),\subseteq\right)$. 
Then $\varphi$ can be represented as $\bigvee\limits_{i<\theta}
\varphi_i$, each $\varphi_i$ is $\kappa$-Souslin.  

\noindent
2) If $\varphi$ is co-$\kappa$-Souslin, then it can be represented as
$\bigvee\limits_{i<\kappa^+} \varphi_i$, each $\varphi_i$ 
is $\kappa$-Borel (i.e. can be obtained from clopen sets 
by unions and intersections of size $\le \kappa$).
<!-- end fact-3 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
) Easy.

\noindent
2) Let $\neg \varphi$ be represented as $\mathrm{prj} \lim(T)$, $T$ a 
$(2,2,\kappa)$-tree.

Now $\varphi(\eta,\nu)$ iff $T_{(\eta,\nu)} = \{\rho$: for some 
$n,(\eta\restriction n, \nu\restriction n, \rho)\in T\}$ is well
founded which is equivalent to the existence of $\alpha< \kappa^+$, $f:
T_{(\eta, \nu)} \rightarrow \alpha$ such that $\rho_1< \rho_2\in T
\Rightarrow f(\rho_1)> f(\rho_2)$. For each $\alpha< \kappa^+$, this
property is $\kappa$-Borel.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- conclusion-6: body_md -->
\label{4.8}
If $\varphi$ is an $\aleph_n$-Souslin subset of ${}^\omega 2 \times
{}^\omega 2$ containing a $(\lambda \mathrm{rd}_{\omega_1} (\aleph_n),
\lambda \mathrm{rd}_{\omega_1}(\aleph_n))$-rectangle, \underline{then} it contains a
perfect rectangle. (Note: $\aleph_n$ can replaced by $\kappa$ if
$\mathrm{cf}\!(S_{\le \aleph_0}(\kappa),\subseteq) = \kappa$, e.g.
$\aleph_{\omega_4}$, by \cite[Ch.IX,\S4]{Sh:g}.)
<!-- end conclusion-6 -->
<!-- conclusion-7: body_md -->
\label{4.9}
1) For $\ell<6,\mathrm{Prk}^\ell_\infty (\kappa^+,(2^{\kappa^+})^+;\kappa)$.

\noindent
2) If $\mathbf{V}= \mathbf{V}_0^\mathbf{P}$,
$\mathbf{P} \models$ c.c.c. and $\mathbf{V}_0 \models \mathrm{GCH}$, \underline{then} 
$\mathrm{Prk}_{\omega_1}(\aleph_1,\aleph_3)$.
<!-- end conclusion-7 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
) For a model $M$, letting $(\lambda_1,\lambda_2)=
(\kappa^+,(2^{\kappa^+})^+)$ choose (for $m=1,2$)
$\bar{a}^m_i$ a nonempty sequence from $R^M_m$ for $i<\lambda_m$,
$\{\bar{a}_i^m:i<\lambda_m\}$ pairwise disjoint. For
$(i,j)\in \lambda_1\times\lambda_2$ let
$\beta_{i,j} = \mathrm{rkk}(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j,M)$ with witnesses
$k^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j)$, $\varphi^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j)$ for
$\neg \mathrm{rkk}^1(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j,M) > \beta_{i,j}$. As
$\lambda_2=(2^{\lambda_1})^+$, $|\tau(M)| \le \kappa$, for some
$B_2 \subseteq \lambda_2$, $|B_2|=\lambda_2$ and for every $i<\lambda_1$ the
following does not depend on $j\in B_2$:

\[
k^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j),\varphi^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j).
\]

\medskip\noindent
Similarly, there is $B_1\subseteq\lambda_1$, $|B_1|=\lambda_1 (=\kappa^+)$
such that for $j= \min( B_2)$ the values

\[
k^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j),\varphi^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j)
\]

\medskip\noindent
are the same for all $i\in B_1$; but they do not depend on $j\in B_2$ either.
So for $(i,j)\in B_1\times B_2$ we have $k^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j)=k^*$,
$\varphi^M(\bar{a}^1_i,\bar{a}^2_j)=\varphi^*$. Let $k^*$ ``speak" on
$\bar{a}^1_i$, for definiteness only. Choose distinct $i_\zeta$ in $B_1$
(for $\zeta<\kappa^+$). Without loss of generality, 
$\mathrm{rkk}^1(\bar{a}^1_{i_0},\bar{a}^1_j,M) \le \mathrm{rkk}^1(\bar{a}^1_{i_\zeta},
\bar{a}^1_j,M)$. 

Now $\bar{a}^1_\zeta$ give contradiction to
$\mathrm{rkk}^l(\bar{a}^1_{i_0},\bar{a}^2_j,M) \not\ge > \beta_{i_0,j}$.

\noindent
2) This can be proved directly (or see \cite{Sh:532} through 
preservation by c.c.c. forcing notion of rank which are relations of 
rkrc similarly to \ref{1.8}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
\label{4.9A}
1) If $T$ is an $(\omega,\omega)$-tree and $A\times B\subseteq\lim(T)$,
with $A,B\subseteq {}^\omega\omega$ uncountable (or just not
scattered) then $\lim(T)$ contains a perfect rectangle.
                                                              
Instead $\lim(T)$ (i.e. a closed set) we can use countable intersection
of open sets. The proof is just like \ref{1.14}.

\noindent
2) We can define a rank for $(2, 2, \kappa)$-trees measuring whether
$\mathrm{prj}\lim (T)\subseteq  {}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$ contains a
perfect rectangle, and similarly for $(\omega, \omega)$-tree $T$
measuring whether $\lim(T)$ contains a perfect rectangle. We then have
theorems parallel to those of \S1. See below and in \cite{Sh:532}.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
\label{4.10}
For $T$ a $(\omega,\omega)$-tree we define a function 
$\mathrm{degrc}_{T}$ (rectangle degree).
Its domain is $\mathrm{rcpr}({T}) := \{(u_1,u_2):$ for
some $\ell < \omega,u_1, u_2$ are finite nonempty subsets of ${}^\ell \omega$ 
and $g$ a function from $u_1\times u_2$ to $\omega$ such that
$(\eta_0,\eta_1)\in T$ for $\eta_i\in u_i\}$. Its
value is an ordinal $\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_1,u_2)$ (or $-1$ or $\infty$). For
this we define the truth value of $\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_1,u_2) \ge \alpha$ by
induction on the ordinal $\alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 1}:   $\alpha=-1$

$\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_1,u_2)\ge -1$ \underline{iff} $(u_1,u_2)$ is in $\mathrm{rcpr}({T})$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 2}:   $\alpha$ limit

$\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_1,u_2) \ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} $\mathrm{degrc}_T(u_1,u_2) \ge \beta$ for
every $\beta < \alpha$.
\bigskip

\noindent
\underline{Case 3}:   $\alpha= \beta +1$

$\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_1,u_2)\ge \alpha$ \underline{iff} for $k\in \{1,2\},\quad \eta^*\in
u_k$ we can find $\ell(*)< \omega$, and functions $h_0,h_1,$ such that:
$\mathrm{Dom} (h_i)=u_1\cup u_2,\quad [\eta \in u_1\cup u_2\Rightarrow
\eta \triangleleft h_i(\eta )\in {}^{\ell(*)}\omega]$ such that 
$h_0(\eta^*)\ne h_1(\eta^*)$, $\eta\in u_{1-k} 
\Rightarrow h_0(\eta)=h_1(\eta)$ and letting 
$u^1_i = \mathrm{Rang}(h_0\restriction u_i)\cup \mathrm{Rang}(h_1\restriction u_i)$ we have
$\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u^1_0,u^1_1)\ge \beta$.

Lastly define: $\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_0,u_1) = \alpha$ \underline{iff} $\bigwedge\limits_\beta
[\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_0,u_1) \ge \beta \Leftrightarrow \alpha\ge \beta]$ 
($\alpha$ an ordinal or $\infty$).
                                                                

Also $\mathrm{degrc} (T)=\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(\{\langle\rangle\}, \{\langle\rangle\})$.
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- claim-23: body_md -->
\label{4.11}
Assume $T$ is in an $(\omega,\omega)$-tree.

\noindent
1) For every $(u_0,u_1) \in \mathrm{rcpr} ({T})$, $\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_0,u_1)$ is
an ordinal or $\infty$ or $-1$; \underline{iff}$f$ is an automorphism of 
$({}^{\omega >} \omega,\triangleleft)$, \underline{then} 
$\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_0,u_1)= \mathrm{degrc}_{f({T})} (f(u_0), f(u_1))$.

\noindent
2) $\mathrm{drc} (T)=\infty$ \underline{iff} there is a perfect
rectangle in $\lim (T)$ \underline{iff} $\mathrm{degrc}_{T}(u_0,u_1)\ge \omega_1$ for some
$(u_0,u_1)$ (so those statements are absolute).

\noindent
3)  If $\mathrm{drc} (T)=\alpha(*)< \omega_1$, \underline{then} $\lim(T)$ contains no 
$(\lambda \mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0),\lambda
\mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0))$-rectangle.

\noindent
4)  If $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu= \langle T_n: n<\omega\rangle$ is a sequence of
$(\omega, \omega)$-trees and $\mathrm{degrc} (T_n) \le \alpha(*)$, and
$A=\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega} \lim(T_n)$ \underline{then}
$A$ contains no $(\lambda \mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0),
\lambda \mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)+1}(\aleph_0)$-rectangle.

\noindent
5)  In part 4. we can replace $\omega$ by any infinite cardinal $\theta$.
<!-- end claim-23 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
), 2), 3) Left to the reader.

\noindent
4) Follows from part 5).

\noindent
5) Let $\lambda = \lambda \mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)+1}(\theta)$, and let $\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu=\langle
T_i: i<\theta\rangle$, $\mathrm{degrc}(T_i) \le \alpha(*)$ and
$A = \bigcup\limits_{i<\theta} \lim(T_i)$. Let $\{\eta_\alpha:
\alpha<\lambda\} \times \{\nu_\beta:\beta< \lambda\}\subseteq A$
where $\alpha< \beta \Rightarrow \eta_\alpha \ne \eta_\beta$ and
$\nu_\alpha \ne \nu_\beta$, and for simplicity $\{\eta_\alpha: \alpha<
\lambda\}\cap \{\nu_\beta:\beta<\lambda\}=\varnothing$. 

We define a model $M$, with universe ${\mathscr{H}}((2^{\aleph_0})^+)$ and
relation: all those definable in $({\mathscr{H}}((2^{\aleph_1})^+), \in,
<^*, R_1, R_2, g, \mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu, i)_{i \le \theta}$ where 
$R^M_1 = \{\eta_\alpha: \alpha< \lambda\}$, 
$R^M_2 = \{\nu_\beta : \beta < \lambda\}$, 
$g(\eta_\alpha, \nu_\beta) = 
\min\{i \le \theta : (\eta_\alpha, \nu_\beta)\in \lim(T_i)\}$.

Next we prove 
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(*)$]  if $w_\ell \in [R^M_\ell]$ for $\ell=1,2$, \underline{then}

\begin{equation*}
\begin{array}{clcr}
\mathrm{rkk}(\langle w_1,w_2\rangle,M) \le \min\{&\mathrm{degrc}_{T_i}(\{\eta_\alpha
\restriction k:\alpha \in u_1\},\{\nu_\beta\restriction k:
\beta\in u_2\}):\\
  &u_1\subseteq w_1,\ u_2\subseteq w_2,\ u_1 \ne \varnothing,\ u_2 \ne \varnothing,
k < \omega \text{ and} \\
  &\langle \eta_\alpha\restriction k:\alpha\in u_1\rangle 
\text{ is with no repetitions, and } \\
  &\langle\nu_\beta\restriction k:\beta\in u_2\rangle 
\text{ is with no repetitions}\}.
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
We prove $(*)$ by induction on the left side of the inequality. Now by
the definitions we are done.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- claim-24: body_md -->
\label{4.15}
1)  For each $\alpha(*)< \omega_1$, there is an $\omega$-sequence 
$\bar{T}=\langle T_n: n< \omega \rangle$ of $(\omega,\;\omega)$-trees 
such that:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item[$(\alpha)$] for every $\mu < \lambda \mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)}
  (\aleph_0)$, some c.c.c. forcing notion adds a $(\mu,\mu)$-rectangle
  to $\bigcup\limits_{n<\omega}\lim (T_n)$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item[$(\beta)$]  $\mathrm{degrc} (T_n)=\alpha(*)$.
\end{enumerate}
\medskip\noindent
2)  If $\mathrm{NPrk}_{\alpha(*)}(\lambda_1,\lambda_2;\aleph_0)$ \underline{then} for some
$(\omega,\omega)$-tree $T$: some c.c.c. forcing notion adds a
$(\lambda_1,\lambda_2)$-rectangle to $\lim(T)$ such that $\alpha(*)
=\mathrm{degrc} (\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muT\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu)$ (consequently, if $\mathrm{Prk}_{\alpha(*)}
(\lambda_1',\lambda_1';\aleph_0)$ then there is no 
$(\lambda_1',\lambda_1')$--rectangle in $\lim (T)$).

\noindent
3)  Moreover, we can have for the tree $T$ of $(4)$: if $\mu <
\lambda \mathrm{rc}_{\alpha(*)} (\aleph_0)$, $A,\;B$ disjoint subsets of 
${}^\omega 2 \times {}^\omega 2$ of cardinality $\le \mu$, \underline{then} 
some c.c.c. forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$, adds an automorphism 
$f$ of $({}^{\omega >}\omega,\triangleleft)$ such that: 
$A\subseteq \lim^* [f(T)],\;\; B\cap \lim^*[f(T)]=\varnothing$ 
(the $\lim^*$ means closure under finite changes).
<!-- end claim-24 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
) We define the forcing for part 2. and delay the others to \cite{Sh:532}.

\noindent
2) It is enough to do it for successor $\alpha(*)$, say $\beta(*)+1$. It
is like \ref{1.10}; we will give the basic definition and the 
new points. Let $M$ be a model as in Definition \ref{4.1}, 
$|R^M_\ell| = \lambda_\ell,\mathrm{rkk}^1(M)<\alpha(*)$
so $\mathrm{rkk}^1(M) \le \beta(*)$. We assume that $R^M_1, R^M_2$ are disjoint sets
of ordinals. For non empty $\bar{a}_l\subseteq R^M_l$ ($l<2$; no
repetition inside $\bar a_l$), let $\varphi^M(\bar{a}_1,
\bar{a}_2)$, $k^M(\bar{a}_1,\bar{a}_2)\in
\mathrm{Rang}(\bar{a}_1)\cup\mathrm{Rang}(\bar{a}_2)$ be witnesses to the value of 
$\mathrm{rkk}^1(\bar{a}_1,\bar{a}_2,M)$ which is $< \alpha(*)$. 

We define the forcing notion $\mathbf{P}$: a condition $p$ consists of:
\medskip\noindent
\begin{enumerate}
\item   $\bar{u}^p = \langle u^p_0,u^p_1\rangle$ and
$u^p_\varepsilon = u_\varepsilon[p]$ is a finite subset of $R^M_\varepsilon$ for
$\varepsilon<2$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item   $n^p = n[p] < \omega$ and 
$\eta^p_\alpha = \eta_\alpha[p]\in{}^{n[p]} \omega$ for
$\alpha\in u^p_\varepsilon$ such that $\alpha \ne \beta\in u^p_\varepsilon
\Rightarrow\ \eta^p_\alpha \ne \eta^p_\beta$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item  $0<m^p<\omega$ and $t^p_m\subseteq
\bigcup\{{}^\ell \omega \times {}^\ell \omega: \ell \le
n^p\}$ closed under initial segments and such that the
$\triangleleft$-maximal elements have the length $n^p$ and
$\langle\rangle\in t^p$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item  the domain of $f^p$ is $\{\bar{u}=(u_1,u_2):$ for some 
$\ell= \ell(u_1, u_2) \le n^p$ and $m=m(u_1,u_2)<m^p$ we have 
$u_\varepsilon \subseteq t^p_\varepsilon \cap{}^\ell \omega$  and if $\alpha_1\in
u_1,\alpha_2\in u_2$, $\eta^p_{\alpha_1}\restriction l\in u_1$,
$\eta^p_{\alpha_2}\restriction l\in u_2$ then $g^p(\alpha_1,\alpha_2)=m\}$
and $f^p(\bar{u})=(f^p_0(\bar{u}),f^p_1(\bar{u}),f^p_2(\bar{u}))\in
\alpha(*)\times (u_1\cup u_2)\times L_{\omega_1,\omega}(\tau(M))$,
\item a function $g^p: u^p_1 \times
u^p_2\rightarrow\{0,\ldots,m^p-1\}$, $m^p<\omega$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item   $t^p_m \cap {}^{(n^p)}\omega= \{(\eta^p_\alpha, \eta^p_\beta):
\alpha\in u^p_0, \beta\in u^p_1$ and $m=g^p(\alpha,\beta)\}$
\smallskip\noindent
\item  \underline{if} $\varnothing \ne u_\varepsilon \subseteq t_\varepsilon \cap {}^\ell \omega$,
$f^p(u_1,u_2)=(\beta^*,\rho^*,\varphi^*)$, $\ell < \ell(*) \le 
n^p$, for $i=0,1$ a function $e_{i,\varepsilon}$ has the domain $u_\varepsilon$, 
$[(\forall l)(\rho\in u_\varepsilon \Rightarrow \rho\triangleleft
e_{i,\varepsilon}(\rho)\in t^p_\varepsilon\cap {}^{\ell(*)} \omega)]$,
$[\rho^*\notin u_\varepsilon$ and $\rho\in u_\varepsilon \Rightarrow e_{0,\varepsilon} 
(\rho)=e_{1,\varepsilon}(\rho)]$,$[\rho^*\in u_\varepsilon \Rightarrow 
e_{0,\varepsilon}(\rho^*)\ne e_{1,\varepsilon}(\rho^*)]$ and 
$f^p(e_{0,1}(u_1)\cup e_{1,1}(u_1),e_{0,2}(u_2)\cup e_{1,2}(u_2))
=(\beta',\rho',\varphi')$ (so well defined), \underline{then}
$\beta'<\beta^*$,
\smallskip\noindent
\item  \underline{if} $\ell \le n^p$, for $\varepsilon =1,2$ we have 
$u_\varepsilon \subseteq u^p_\varepsilon$ are non empty, the sequence 
$\langle \eta^p_\alpha\restriction \ell: \alpha\in u_\varepsilon\rangle$ is 
with no repetition,  and $u_\varepsilon' = \{\eta^p_\alpha \restriction \ell:
\alpha\in u_\varepsilon\}$ and $f^p(u_1',u_2')$ is well defined, \underline{then}
\begin{enumerate}
    \item $f^p_2(u_1',u_2')=\varphi^M(u_1,u_2)$,

    \item $f^p_1(u_1',u_2')=\eta^p_\alpha\restriction l$ 

    \item where $\alpha$ is $k^M(u_1,u_2)$ and 

    \item $f^p_0(u_1',u_2',h)=\mathrm{rkk}(u_1,u_2,M)$,

\end{enumerate}

\smallskip\noindent
\item  if $(u'_1,u'_2)\in \mathrm{Dom}(f^p)$ \underline{then} there are $l,u_1,u_2$
as above, 
\smallskip\noindent
\item  if $(\eta_1,\eta_2)\in t^p_m \cap ({}^{n}2 \times{}^n2)$ \underline{then} for
some $\alpha_1\in u^p_1$, $\alpha_2\in u^p_2$ we have 
$g^p(\alpha_1,\alpha_2)=m$ and $\eta_1\trianglelefteq\eta^p_{\alpha_1}$,
$\eta_2\trianglelefteq\eta^p_{\alpha_2}$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
\label{4.11B}
We can generalize \ref{4.10}, \ref{4.11}, 
  \ref{4.15}                                         
  to Souslin
relations.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
